1. Context
A fleet operator with 500 electric trucks needs one screen answering: where are my vehicles, are they okay, whatneeds attention now.

### Claude — 2026-09-12T06:54:04.625Z

```
Bash
{
  "command": "mkdir -p /Users/durgeshparekh/Documents/GitHub/byte_beam_project && cat > /Users/durgeshparekh/Documents/GitHub/byte_beam_project/ARCHITECTURE.md <<'DOC'\n# Fleet Console \u2014 System Design\n\nFlutter take-home, Bytebeam SDE-3. Local-first fleet telemetry console over embedded DuckDB.\n\nStatus: **design only, nothing built yet.**\n\n---\n\n## 0. The one idea\n\n> **Derived state is a pure fold over an append-only event log. Late or duplicate data is handled by replaying the affected suffix, never by patching.**\n\nEverything hard in this brief \u2014 duplicates, out-of-order packets, GPS jitter, backlog dumps, geofence edits, trip revision \u2014 collapses into that sentence. Alerts, geofence transitions and trips are *never* mutated in place by an incoming packet. They are recomputed from the log for the window the packet touches. Recompute is idempotent because the derivation is a pure function of `(event log \u2229 [t0, \u221e), config)`.\n\nThe corollary is the invariant worth testing above all others:\n\n> Shuffle the packet stream arbitrarily, replay duplicates, and the final derived state must be byte-identical to the in-order replay.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Layout\n\n```\nlib/\n  db/          schema.sql \u00b7 migrations \u00b7 DuckDbService (isolate, connections)\n  ingest/      PacketSource (simulator) \u00b7 IngestPipeline \u00b7 Backfill\n  derive/      SQL derivation: latest \u00b7 alerts \u00b7 transitions \u00b7 trips\n  query/       read-side queries returning plain Dart records\n  features/    fleet \u00b7 vehicle \u00b7 alerts \u00b7 geofences \u00b7 trips  (Riverpod + widgets)\ntool/\n  bench.dart           cold start / p50 / p95 / memory harness\n  export_ai_logs.sh    Claude session jsonl -> ai-logs/*.md\nai-logs/       uncurated AI conversation logs (deliverable 3)\n```\n\nNo repository interfaces, no DAO layer, no abstract `TelemetryService`. One implementation each; a swap is a constructor argument, not an abstraction. Query functions return records/freezed models straight from SQL.\n\n### Isolates\n\nDuckDB is a single-writer, MVCC embedded engine. One process, so multiple connections against one instance are fine \u2014 readers get a snapshot while the writer commits.\n\n- **Writer isolate** \u2014 owns ingest, derivation, backfill. Everything that mutates runs here, serialised, in transactions.\n- **UI isolate** \u2014 read-only connection, queries on demand and on a *debounced* change ping (250 ms). A 3-hour backlog dump must not repaint the fleet list 500 times.\n\n`dart_duckdb` exposes a transferable database handle for exactly this. Ingest batches are bounded (\u2264 5k rows / 200 ms per transaction) so a backlog dump never holds a write lock long enough to stall reads.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Schema\n\nLocation is deliberately **not** a signal. Pairing `lat` and `lon` rows by timestamp is pointless work, and every geofence query wants them side by side.\n\n```sql\nCREATE TABLE vehicle (\n  vehicle_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,\n  reg_no     TEXT NOT NULL,\n  model      TEXT NOT NULL\n);\n\n-- append-only event log; the source of truth\nCREATE TABLE signal_reading (\n  vehicle_id TEXT NOT NULL,\n  signal     TEXT NOT NULL,        -- soc | speed | battery_temp | odometer | range_km | ignition\n  event_ts   TIMESTAMP NOT NULL,   -- from the packet\n  value      DOUBLE  NOT NULL,     -- booleans as 0/1\n  ingest_ts  TIMESTAMP NOT NULL,   -- audit only, never drives logic\n  PRIMARY KEY (vehicle_id, signal, event_ts)\n);\n\nCREATE TABLE location_fix (\n  vehicle_id  TEXT NOT NULL,\n  event_ts    TIMESTAMP NOT NULL,\n  lat DOUBLE NOT NULL, lon DOUBLE NOT NULL,\n  accuracy_m  DOUBLE,\n  ingest_ts   TIMESTAMP NOT NULL,\n  PRIMARY KEY (vehicle_id, event_ts)\n);\n\n-- 500 vehicles x ~6 signals = ~3k rows. The whole read path lives here.\nCREATE TABLE vehicle_signal_latest (\n  vehicle_id TEXT NOT NULL,\n  signal     TEXT NOT NULL,\n  event_ts   TIMESTAMP NOT NULL,\n  value      DOUBLE NOT NULL,\n  PRIMARY KEY (vehicle_id, signal)\n);\n\n-- config as data, not as Dart constants: the UI, the verdict pills and the\n-- alert evaluator all read the same row.\nCREATE TABLE signal_spec (\n  signal TEXT PRIMARY KEY,\n  label TEXT, unit TEXT,\n  max_age_sec INTEGER,      -- older than this => STALE\n  warn_lo DOUBLE, warn_hi DOUBLE,\n  crit_lo DOUBLE, crit_hi DOUBLE\n);\n\nCREATE TABLE geofence (\n  geofence_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,\n  name TEXT NOT NULL,\n  lat DOUBLE, lon DOUBLE, radius_m DOUBLE,\n  active_from TIMESTAMP NOT NULL,     -- activation is time-versioned\n  active_to   TIMESTAMP,              -- NULL = still active; deactivated fences are retained\n  updated_at  TIMESTAMP NOT NULL\n);\n\nCREATE TABLE alert (\n  alert_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,          -- deterministic: hash(vehicle_id, type, raised_at)\n  vehicle_id TEXT NOT NULL,\n  alert_type TEXT NOT NULL,           -- battery_low (escalating) | battery_overheat\n  severity   TEXT NOT NULL,           -- warning | critical\n  raised_at  TIMESTAMP NOT NULL,      -- event time\n  escalated_at TIMESTAMP,\n  resolved_at  TIMESTAMP,\n  dismissed_at TIMESTAMP,\n  dismiss_reason TEXT\n);\n\n-- derived, fully recomputable\nCREATE TABLE geofence_transition (\n  vehicle_id TEXT, geofence_id TEXT,\n  event_ts TIMESTAMP,                 -- time of the crossing fix, not the confirming fix\n  kind TEXT,                          -- ENTRY | EXIT\n  confidence TEXT,                    -- high | low (crossed a data gap)\n  PRIMARY KEY (vehicle_id, geofence_id, event_ts)\n);\n\nCREATE TABLE trip (\n  trip_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,           -- deterministic: hash(vehicle_id, start_ts)\n  vehicle_id TEXT NOT NULL,\n  origin_geofence_id TEXT, start_ts TIMESTAMP NOT NULL,\n  dest_geofence_id   TEXT, end_ts   TIMESTAMP,\n  status TEXT NOT NULL,               -- IN_PROGRESS | COMPLETED\n  distance_km DOUBLE,                 -- odometer delta, NULL if unavailable\n  confidence TEXT\n);\n\nCREATE TABLE ingest_watermark (\n  vehicle_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,\n  processed_through TIMESTAMP NOT NULL\n);\n```\n\n`signal_reading` is the only large table. Its 3-column ART primary key is what makes duplicate rejection O(log n) instead of a table scan \u2014 and it is the first thing I will look at if memory at rest disappoints (see \u00a77).\n\n---\n\n## 3. Ingest\n\nOne transaction per batch, in the writer isolate:\n\n1. **Dedupe within the batch** \u2014 keep `max(event_ts)` per `(vehicle_id, signal)`.\n2. **Append** \u2014 `INSERT \u2026 ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`. Idempotency is now a database constraint, not a code path.\n3. **Advance latest** \u2014 the only tricky upsert. A late packet must never clobber a newer reading:\n\n```sql\nINSERT INTO vehicle_signal_latest AS l (vehicle_id, signal, event_ts, value)\nSELECT vehicle_id, signal, event_ts, value FROM batch\nON CONFLICT (vehicle_id, signal) DO UPDATE\n  SET value = excluded.value, event_ts = excluded.event_ts\n  WHERE excluded.event_ts > l.event_ts;\n```\n\n4. **Detect regression** \u2014 if `min(event_ts)` in the batch for a vehicle is older than its `ingest_watermark`, that vehicle needs a replay from `min(event_ts) \u2212 lookback`. Otherwise, incremental derivation from the watermark forward.\n5. **Derive** \u2014 alerts, transitions, trips (\u00a75, \u00a76).\n6. **Advance watermark**, commit, ping the UI.\n\nDerivation runs **in SQL**, not Dart. Pulling the log into Dart to fold over it is precisely the in-memory-shadow the brief rules out, and it makes recompute over a large window unaffordable. The rules (hysteresis, debounce, thresholds) live in config tables so tests can vary them without touching SQL.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Fleet list\n\nThe whole read path is ~3k rows, so the query is trivially fast and no caching is warranted.\n\n```sql\nWITH p AS (\n  SELECT vehicle_id,\n         max(event_ts)                                   AS last_ping,\n         max(value)    FILTER (signal = 'soc')           AS soc,\n         max(event_ts) FILTER (signal = 'soc')           AS soc_ts,\n         max(value)    FILTER (signal = 'speed')         AS speed,\n         max(event_ts) FILTER (signal = 'speed')         AS speed_ts,\n         max(value)    FILTER (signal = 'ignition')      AS ignition,\n         max(event_ts) FILTER (signal = 'ignition')      AS ignition_ts,\n         max(value)    FILTER (signal = 'range_km')      AS range_km\n  FROM vehicle_signal_latest GROUP BY vehicle_id\n)\nSELECT v.reg_no, v.model, p.soc, p.range_km,\n  CASE\n    WHEN p.last_ping IS NULL OR p.last_ping < $now - INTERVAL 10 MINUTE THEN 'OFFLINE'\n    WHEN p.speed    > 0 AND p.speed_ts    >= $now - INTERVAL 5 MINUTE   THEN 'MOVING'\n    WHEN p.speed    = 0 AND p.speed_ts    >= $now - INTERVAL 5 MINUTE\n     AND p.ignition = 1 AND p.ignition_ts >= $now - INTERVAL 5 MINUTE   THEN 'IDLE'\n    ELSE 'STOPPED'\n  END AS status\nFROM vehicle v LEFT JOIN p USING (vehicle_id);\n```\n\n`last_ping` is `max(event_ts)` over all signals **and** location fixes for that vehicle. Filter chip counts are `GROUP BY status` over the same CTE \u2014 one query, computed in SQL, never in Dart.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Alerts\n\nEvaluated over `vehicle_signal_latest \u22c8 signal_spec`, **fresh readings only**, after every ingest batch.\n\n| Alert | Condition | Severity |\n|---|---|---|\n| `battery_low` | SOC < 20 % | warning |\n| `battery_low` | SOC < 10 % | **critical** (same row, escalated) |\n| `battery_overheat` | battery_temp > 45 \u00b0C | critical |\n\nThe two SOC alerts are one row whose `severity` moves warning \u21c4 critical. Recovering from 8 % to 15 % de-escalates in place; it does not resolve and re-raise.\n\n**Dismissal.** Sheet order is fixed: *I am on it* \u00b7 *Wrong alert* \u00b7 *Something else\u2026*. The dismissal is written to DuckDB immediately and UNDO clears `dismissed_at`. It is not held in memory for 5 seconds \u2014 local-first means the database is the truth, and if the app dies mid-window the dismissal stands. That is the honest trade; the alternative loses a user's explicit action to a crash.\n\n**Resolution is independent.** The evaluator clears an alert when its condition clears, dismissed or not. A dismissed alert whose condition never clears simply stays hidden until the condition clears and re-triggers \u2014 dismissal suppresses the *episode*, not the *rule*.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Geofences and trips\n\n### 6.1 Transition detection\n\nDeterministic pipeline, applied per `(vehicle, geofence)` over event-time-ordered fixes:\n\n1. **Accuracy gate** \u2014 discard fixes with `accuracy_m > 100`. Filtered at read; nothing is ever deleted from the log.\n2. **Hysteresis** \u2014 inside if `d \u2264 r \u2212 h`, outside if `d \u2265 r + h`, with `h = max(25 m, accuracy_m)`. Fixes in the band produce **no zone opinion**.\n3. **Carry forward** \u2014 `last_value(zone IGNORE NULLS) OVER (PARTITION BY vehicle_id, geofence_id ORDER BY event_ts)`. The band is now genuinely inert rather than a source of flapping.\n4. **Confirm** \u2014 a crossing is confirmed when two consecutive opinionated fixes agree on the new zone. Escape hatch for a fast exit: one fix more than `2h` past the boundary confirms alone.\n5. **Timestamp** \u2014 the transition is stamped with the **first** fix of the confirming pair. That is when the vehicle actually crossed.\n6. **Gaps** \u2014 if the confirming pair straddles a gap > 30 min (the basement case), the transition is still recorded but flagged `confidence = 'low'`, and any trip built on it inherits that flag. Nothing is synthesised across a gap.\n7. **Overlaps** \u2014 no single \"current geofence\" internally; containment is per fence. The UI's single-value *current geofence* is the **smallest radius containing the vehicle**, tie-broken by `geofence_id`.\n8. **Fence activation** is time-versioned via `active_from` / `active_to`, so evaluation asks \"was this fence active at the fix's event time\" and recompute stays pure. **Geometry is not versioned** \u2014 editing centre or radius triggers a full recompute of that fence's transitions and the affected trips. Full geometry versioning is the upgrade if fences turn out to be edited often; it is not worth the table for this exercise.\n\nAll of steps 2\u20136 are expressible with `lag`/`last_value IGNORE NULLS` window functions \u2014 set-based, no row-at-a-time Dart fold.\n\n### 6.2 Trips\n\nA nested fence must not manufacture a trip: leaving a bay while still inside the depot is not a departure. So trips key off **containment count**, not individual fences:\n\n```sql\nSELECT *, sum(CASE kind WHEN 'ENTRY' THEN 1 ELSE -1 END)\n            OVER (PARTITION BY vehicle_id ORDER BY event_ts) AS inside_count\nFROM geofence_transition\n```\n\n- `inside_count` falling to **0** \u2192 trip starts; origin = the fence just exited.\n- `inside_count` rising off 0 \u2192 active trip completes; destination = the fence just entered. Returning to origin is a normal completion.\n- Still 0 at the end of the stream \u2192 `IN_PROGRESS`. No timeout; a truck that never reports again keeps an open trip, which is the truthful representation.\n- One active trip per vehicle falls out of the model for free.\n- Incremental recompute seeds `inside_count` from the containment state at the window start.\n\n`distance_km` comes from an `ASOF JOIN` onto the odometer log at `start_ts` and `end_ts`; NULL when the odometer is missing rather than guessed.\n\n**Idempotency.** `trip_id = hash(vehicle_id, start_ts)`, so a duplicate packet regenerates the identical row. A late packet that moves a boundary is handled by the replay in \u00a73 step 4 \u2014 delete derived rows at or after `t0`, re-derive \u2014 with the deterministic id as the backstop, not the mechanism.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Scale\n\n**Backfill.** 500 vehicles \u00d7 6 signals \u00d7 ~700 ticks \u2248 2.1 M rows, generated **inside DuckDB** with `range()` and `random()` in a single `INSERT \u2026 SELECT`. Generating rows in Dart and pushing them across the FFI boundary would take minutes; this takes seconds. The table is created without the primary key, bulk-loaded, then `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX` once \u2014 building the ART index in one pass instead of 2 M times.\n\n**Measurements to report**, on a named physical device plus a named emulator:\n\n| Metric | How | Target |\n|---|---|---|\n| Cold start \u2192 first painted fleet list | stopwatch from `main()`, stopped in `addTimingsCallback` on first frame with data | < 2.5 s |\n| Fleet-list query p50 / p95, warm | 100 runs in the writer isolate, dumped to CSV | p95 < 25 ms |\n| Memory at rest, list open | `adb shell dumpsys meminfo` PSS, DevTools cross-check | < 250 MB |\n\nIf a number is bad it gets reported as bad, with a diagnosis. The two I already expect to be the suspects:\n\n- **Cold start** is dominated by opening the database and loading the ART index, not by the query. Mitigation: open and run the fleet query in the writer isolate before the first frame and paint a skeleton.\n- **Memory at rest** is dominated by that same index on a 2 M-row table. If it dominates, the fix is to drop the primary key and dedupe with an ANTI JOIN against a bounded recent window \u2014 duplicates only ever arrive near the present, so a full-history index earns nothing.\n\n**Retention.** An append-only log grows forever, so:\n\n- `signal_reading` keeps 7 days at full resolution.\n- Older data is downsampled into 5-minute buckets per `(vehicle, signal)` \u2014 min/max/avg/last \u2014 and the raw rows are dropped, followed by `CHECKPOINT`.\n- Derived tables (transitions, trips, alerts) are small and kept indefinitely.\n- **What is lost:** sub-5-minute shape of old data, and the ability to *recompute* geofence transitions outside the hot window. Trip replay is therefore bounded to 7 days; a late packet older than that is rejected and counted, not silently applied.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Tests\n\nThe suite is built around the invariant from \u00a70, not around line coverage.\n\n**The test that matters:** generate a packet stream, shuffle it, inject duplicates and delays, replay it, and assert the derived state \u2014 latest values, alerts, transitions, trips \u2014 is identical to the in-order replay. If that holds, the concurrency and late-data story holds.\n\nAround it, fixture-driven SQL tests against an in-memory DuckDB (fast, no Flutter binding):\n\n- duplicate packet \u00b7 out-of-order packet \u00b7 backlog dump\n- GPS jitter parked on the boundary \u2192 **zero** transitions\n- fast exit confirmed by a single distant fix\n- data gap \u2192 transition present, `confidence = 'low'`\n- nested fences \u2192 exiting the inner bay starts no trip\n- return-to-origin trip completes\n- fence geometry edit \u2192 transitions and trips recomputed\n- fence deactivated \u2192 prior transitions retained, no new ones\n- SOC 18 \u2192 8 escalates in place (one alert row, not two)\n- dismiss then escalate \u2192 alert reappears\n- condition clears while dismissed \u2192 resolved\n- signal goes stale while alerting \u2192 **not** resolved\n- never-reported signal \u2192 \"\u2014\", no pill\n\nPlus widget tests for the fleet list statuses and chip counts, the dismissal sheet ordering and the 5-second undo, and one integration test that backfills 10 vehicles and asserts row counts and query sanity.\n\n---\n\n## 9. Ambiguities and how they are resolved\n\nThe brief says the data model has genuinely ambiguous cases. These are the ones I found and the calls I made.\n\n| # | Ambiguity | Resolution | Rejected alternative |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | Vehicle is online (odometer 2 min old) but `speed` is 40 min old. MOVING? | Status only reads **fresh** signals; a stale speed cannot claim MOVING or IDLE, so it falls through to STOPPED. STOPPED is the documented fallback bucket. | A sixth `UNKNOWN` chip \u2014 the brief specifies five. |\n| 2 | What is \"last ping\"? | `max(event_ts)` across all signals *and* location fixes for the vehicle. | Per-signal freshness only \u2014 the brief says vehicle-level. |\n| 3 | Event time or arrival time? | **Event time** drives every rule: freshness, status, alerts, transitions, trips. `ingest_ts` is audit only. | Arrival time \u2014 makes a backlog dump look like a fleet that just woke up. |\n| 4 | What is a duplicate? | Identity is `(vehicle_id, signal, event_ts)`. Same key, different value \u2192 keep the first, increment a conflict counter surfaced in a debug view. | Last-write-wins \u2014 non-deterministic under reordering, which breaks \u00a70. |\n| 5 | Packet older than the retention window | Rejected and counted. It cannot be replayed correctly, so applying it would produce state that no longer follows from the log. | Silently inserting it and leaving derived state inconsistent. |\n| 6 | Signal goes stale while an alert is open | Alert stays open, shown with a stale marker. | Auto-resolve \u2014 that hides a truck that died at 5 % SOC. |\n| 7 | Dismissed at 18 %, then SOC hits 8 % | Escalation clears `dismissed_at` and re-raises. \"I am on it\" at 18 % is not consent to ignore 8 %. | Staying dismissed through escalation. |\n| 8 | UNDO window vs. app kill | Dismissal is persisted immediately; UNDO clears it. Killed mid-window \u2192 dismissal stands. | Holding it in memory for 5 s \u2014 loses an explicit user action to a crash. |\n| 9 | Geofence edited after history exists | Activation is time-versioned; **geometry is not** \u2014 a geometry edit recomputes that fence's transitions and affected trips. | Full geometry versioning (correct, more table than this earns) or forward-only edits (cheap, but derived state stops being reproducible from the log). |\n| 10 | Vehicle inside two overlapping fences | Containment tracked per fence. UI \"current geofence\" = smallest radius containing it, tie-break `geofence_id`. | A single current-fence column \u2014 undefined under nesting. |\n| 11 | Exiting a bay inside a depot | Trips key off containment count reaching 0, so leaving the inner fence starts nothing. | Per-fence exit starts a trip \u2014 one departure would produce two trips. |\n| 12 | Trip whose vehicle never reports again | Stays `IN_PROGRESS` forever. | A 24-hour abandonment timeout \u2014 invents an ending the data does not support. |\n| 13 | GPS jitter on the boundary | 25 m (or accuracy, whichever larger) hysteresis band plus two-fix confirmation; band fixes carry the previous zone forward. | Bare `d < r` \u2014 flaps a parked truck into dozens of trips. |\n\n---\n\n## 10. Scope cuts\n\nStated up front, per the brief.\n\n- **No backend, no auth, no sync.** The packet source is a simulator behind a one-method interface; a real MQTT/HTTP feed is a constructor swap.\n- **No map tiles in v1.** Geofences are created and edited by name / lat / lon / radius with a schematic canvas preview. `flutter_map` goes in only if time remains after tests \u2014 it is presentation, and every ambiguity above is decided without it.\n- **No background ingest while the app is killed.** No foreground service, no WorkManager.\n- **No severity/escalation audit trail.** The alert row carries `escalated_at`; the full state-change log is a table I would add the moment anyone asked \"why did this fire\".\n- **No i18n, no custom theme.** Material 3 defaults.\n\n---\n\n## 11. Deliverables plan\n\n**Commits** \u2014 phased, in my own words, no squash: schema \u2192 ingest + dedupe \u2192 fleet list \u2192 vehicle detail + verdicts \u2192 alerts \u2192 geofences \u2192 transitions \u2192 trips \u2192 backfill + bench \u2192 tests \u2192 README.\n\n**README** \u2014 run the app, run the tests, 30-second feature tour, plus the measured numbers from \u00a77 and the device they came from.\n\n**AI logs** \u2014 `ai-logs/`, uncurated, committed as work proceeds so the dead ends stay in the history rather than being tidied away at the end. `tool/export_ai_logs.sh` converts the raw Claude Code session transcripts (`~/.claude/projects/<slug>/*.jsonl`) into chronological markdown. Two practical notes: run Claude from the project root so its sessions land in one slug directory, and export after every session \u2014 the transcripts are the deliverable, and a curated summary is explicitly not what was asked for.\nDOC\nwc -l /Users/durgeshparekh/Documents/GitHub/byte_beam_project/ARCHITECTURE.md",
  "description": "Write the architecture design doc"
}

### Claude — 2026-09-12T06:55:37.232Z
Read the PDF (had to un-ASCII85 it — no poppler on this box). Design written.

**[ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md)** — 11 sections. The spine:

**One idea:** derived state is a pure fold over the append-only log. Late/duplicate/reordered packets are handled by replaying the affected suffix, never by patching. Alerts, transitions and trips all fall out of that, and it gives you the one test worth writing: *shuffle the stream, inject duplicates, assert the final state equals in-order replay.*

**Concrete calls:**
- `signal_reading` PK `(vehicle, signal, event_ts)` → dedupe is a DB constraint, not a code path. Location gets its own table, not two paired signals.
- `vehicle_signal_latest` (~3k rows) is the entire read path — fleet list + chip counts are one SQL query over it, with a conditional `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE … WHERE excluded.event_ts > l.event_ts` so late packets can't clobber.
- Derivation in SQL window functions (`last_value … IGNORE NULLS`, `lag`, ASOF join), not a Dart fold — a Dart fold *is* the in-memory shadow the brief forbids.
- Jitter: 25 m hysteresis band + two-fix confirmation; band fixes carry the previous zone forward. Trips key off **containment count hitting 0**, not per-fence exits — so leaving a bay inside a depot starts nothing, and "one active trip" is free.
- Backfill generates 2.1 M rows *inside* DuckDB via `range()`, index built after load.

**§9 is the section they're actually grading** — 13 ambiguities in a table with the call and the rejected alternative (stale speed → STOPPED; stale signal does *not* resolve an alert; escalation un-dismisses; geometry not versioned but activation is; late packet past retention rejected and counted).

Also added **[tool/export_ai_logs.sh](tool/export_ai_logs.sh)** — dumps Claude session jsonl → `ai-logs/*.md`, uncurated, tool calls included. Smoke-tested against a real transcript dir (34 turns out clean). Run it from the project root or the slug won't match.

skipped: map tiles, backend, escalation audit trail — all listed in §10 with when to add. Two `dart_duckdb` assumptions to verify in the first spike: transferable DB across isolates, and `ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE … WHERE`.

Waiting for your instruction. Want this as a shareable page too, or is the repo file enough?

The design question is **who owns the truth for each row**, because that one classification decides the sync direction, the conflict rule, the transaction boundary, and whether the table can simply be thrown away and rebuilt. Every table in \u00a72 belongs to exactly one of three classes.\n\n| Class | Tables | Origin | Sync | Conflict | On migration / loss |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **R** \u2014 remote-authoritative, immutable | `vehicle`, `signal_reading`, `location_fix` | the trucks | pull, cursored | impossible by construction | re-pullable |\n| **L** \u2014 device-authored intent | `geofence`, `alert.dismissed_at`/`dismiss_reason` | the operator, on this device | push, via outbox | per-table rule, never a blanket policy | **the only data that can actually be lost** |\n| **D** \u2014 derived | `vehicle_signal_latest`, `geofence_transition`, `trip`, alert raise/resolve state | this device, from R + L | never synced | n/a | dropped and rebuilt |\n\n### 3.1 Class R \u2014 append-only, natural key, cursored pull\n\nRows are immutable and identified by a natural key (`(vehicle_id, signal, event_ts)`, `(vehicle_id, event_ts)`), written with `INSERT \u2026 ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, and the pull cursor advances **in the same transaction as the rows it covers**.\n\nThat combination buys the property offline-first actually needs: **at-least-once delivery is sufficient, because the write is idempotent.** No exactly-once protocol, no ack dance. Crash between fetch and commit? Re-pull the page; the constraint absorbs it. Dedupe is the *side effect* of the natural key, not its purpose.\n\nClass R is never row-updated. DuckDB is an OLAP engine \u2014 a per-row `UPDATE` or `DELETE` against a 2 M-row table is a rewrite, not an in-place poke. Confining all mutation to tables of a few thousand rows (`vehicle_signal_latest`, `alert`, `geofence`, per-vehicle slices of the derived tables) is not incidental tidiness; it is the reason the schema is shaped this way.\n\n### 3.2 Class L \u2014 the outbox\n\n```sql\nCREATE TABLE outbox (\n  op_id      TEXT PRIMARY KEY,   -- UUIDv7: idempotency key AND total order, client-generated\n  entity     TEXT NOT NULL,      -- geofence | alert_dismissal\n  entity_id  TEXT NOT NULL,\n  op         TEXT NOT NULL,      -- upsert | delete\n  payload    JSON NOT NULL,\n  created_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL,\n  attempts   INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,\n  last_error TEXT,\n  synced_at  TIMESTAMP           -- NULL = pending\n);\n```\n\nThree rules make this work:\n\n1. **The entity row and its outbox row are written in one transaction.** This is the whole reason the outbox exists. \"Saved locally but never queued\" is not a state the database can be in.\n2. **`op_id` is the server's idempotency key.** A retry after an ambiguous timeout is free. UUIDv7 sorts by creation time, so per-entity replay order is the key order \u2014 no sequence table.\n3. **Coalesce pending ops per `entity_id`.** A week in a basement dismissing and un-dismissing the same alert must not queue a week of operations.\n\n**Conflict resolution is per table, not global.** A blanket last-writer-wins is a smell, and the two Class L tables want opposite rules:\n\n- **`geofence` \u2192 LWW on `updated_at`.** A fence has one current shape; the most recent edit is the intended one.\n- **`alert` dismissal \u2192 earliest wins.** A dismissal is a monotonic acknowledgement \u2014 \"someone was on this at T\". LWW would let a device that reconnects later overwrite the fact that a colleague had already picked it up, which is exactly the information the dismissal exists to carry.\n\n### 3.3 Class D \u2014 disposable on purpose\n\nDerived tables are never synced, never backed up, and **never migrated**: any schema change drops and rebuilds them from R + L. That removes them from the migration surface entirely, which is most of the schema. It is also what makes the \u00a70 replay property operationally useful rather than merely elegant \u2014 \"rebuild\" is a supported, exercised code path, not a theory.\n\n### 3.4 Transaction boundaries and crash recovery\n\nIngest is deliberately **two** transactions, not one:\n\n1. append Class R rows \u00b7 advance `vehicle_signal_latest` \u00b7 advance the pull cursor\n2. derive alerts / transitions / trips \u00b7 advance `ingest_watermark`\n\nSplitting them keeps a long recompute from holding a write lock across the whole batch, and it enforces the ordering that matters: **the watermark advances last, so derived state is always behind the log and never ahead of it.**\n\nThe payoff is that there is no recovery routine. A crash between the two transactions leaves log rows with no derivation \u2014 which is byte-identical to the state during normal operation, and is repaired by the same \"derive from watermark\" call that every batch makes. Crash recovery is indistinguishable from ordinary incremental work, so it is tested by every test rather than by a special one nobody runs.\n\n### 3.5 Migrations on a device you cannot reach\n\n`schema_version` table, forward-only, applied on open before any query. The class split does most of the work: **D** is dropped and rebuilt, **R** can be re-pulled from cursor zero if a migration would be gnarly, and **L** \u2014 the only data with no upstream copy \u2014 is small enough to migrate by hand and carefully.\n\n### 3.6 What can actually be lost\n\nOnly unsynced outbox rows, and only if the database file becomes unopenable. Everything in R is re-pullable and everything in D is re-derivable. The mitigation is draining the outbox eagerly whenever connectivity exists, not mirroring it into a second store \u2014 a second store is a second thing to corrupt.\n\n### 3.7 The seam in this build\n\nThere is no backend (\u00a711), so: the cursor mechanism is real, because the packet simulator is a replayable, positioned source and ingest advances a genuine cursor against it. The outbox is **designed, not built** \u2014 it is one table and one drain loop, and until a server exists it would be a queue with no consumer. Swapping the simulator for an HTTP or MQTT source is a constructor argument; adding the outbox is the second commit after that.\n\n---\n\n'''\np = pathlib.Path(\"ARCHITECTURE.md\"); s = p.read_text()\nassert \"## 4. Ingest\" in s\np.write_text(s.replace(\"## 4. Ingest\", NEW + \"## 4. Ingest\", 1))\nPY\ngrep -n '^## ' ARCHITECTURE.md; wc -l ARCHITECTURE.md",
  "description": "Insert offline-first section"

### User — 2026-09-12T07:46:57.543Z
**result**
```
## 4. Ingest

One transaction per batch, in the writer isolate:

1. **Dedupe within the batch** — keep `max(event_ts)` per `(vehicle_id, signal)`.
2. **Append** — `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`. Idempotency is now a database constraint, not a code path.
3. **Advance latest** — the only tricky upsert. A late packet must never clobber a newer reading:

```sql
INSERT INTO vehicle_signal_latest AS l (vehicle_id, signal, event_ts, value)
SELECT vehicle_id, signal, event_ts, value FROM batch
ON CONFLICT (vehicle_id, signal) DO UPDATE
  SET value = excluded.value, event_ts = excluded.event_ts
  WHERE excluded.event_ts > l.event_ts;
```

4. **Detect regression** — if `min(event_ts)` in the batch for a vehicle is older than its `ingest_watermark`, that vehicle needs a replay from `min(event_ts) − lookback`. Otherwise, incremental derivation from the watermark forward.
5. **Derive** — alerts, transitions, trips (§6, §7).
6. **Advance watermark**, commit, ping the UI.

Derivation runs **in SQL**, not Dart. Pulling the log into Dart to fold over it is precisely the in-memory-shadow the brief rules out, and it makes recompute over a large window unaffordable. The rules (hysteresis, debounce, thresholds) live in config tables so tests can vary them without touching SQL.

---

## 5. Fleet list
```

### Claude — 2026-09-12T07:47:21.703Z
Added as **§3 in [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md)** (renumbered the rest; §4 ingest now matches).

The whole answer is one classification: **who owns the truth for each row.** That decides sync direction, conflict rule, transaction boundary, and whether the table is disposable.

| Class | Tables | Sync | Conflict | On loss |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **R** remote-authoritative, immutable | `vehicle`, `signal_reading`, `location_fix` | pull, cursored | impossible by construction | re-pullable |
| **L** device-authored intent | `geofence`, alert dismissal | push, via outbox | per-table rule | **the only losable data** |
| **D** derived | `vehicle_signal_latest`, transitions, trips | never | n/a | dropped and rebuilt |

Four things that fall out of it:

**Natural key + cursor-in-the-same-transaction means at-least-once delivery is enough.** No exactly-once protocol. Crash between fetch and commit → re-pull, the constraint absorbs it. Dedupe is the *side effect* of the natural key, not its purpose.

**Class R is never row-updated.** DuckDB is OLAP — a per-row `UPDATE` on a 2 M-row table is a rewrite. Confining all mutation to the few-thousand-row tables isn't tidiness, it's the reason the schema is shaped this way.

**Outbox: entity row and outbox row in one transaction.** "Saved locally but never queued" stops being a representable state. `op_id` = UUIDv7 = idempotency key *and* replay order. Pending ops coalesce per entity so a week in a basement doesn't queue a week of work. Conflict rules are per-table, deliberately: geofence is LWW on `updated_at`, but **alert dismissal is earliest-wins** — a dismissal is a monotonic acknowledgement, and LWW would let a late-reconnecting device overwrite the fact that a colleague was already on it. A blanket LWW policy is the smell.

**Two transactions per ingest batch, watermark last.** Derived state is always behind the log, never ahead — so there is no recovery routine. A crash mid-batch leaves a state identical to normal operation, repaired by the same "derive from watermark" call every batch makes. Crash recovery gets exercised by every test instead of by a special one nobody runs.

Class D also drops out of the migration surface entirely (drop + rebuild), leaving only the small Class L tables needing real migrations.

skipped: the outbox is designed, not built — no backend in this scope (§11), so it'd be a queue with no consumer. The cursor is real, because the simulator is a positioned replayable source. Add the outbox the commit after a server exists.

=== duckdb ===
import 'package:dart_duckdb/src/api/connection.dart';
import 'package:dart_duckdb/src/api/database.dart';

abstract class DuckDB {
  Object? get bindings;

  Future<Database> open(String filename, {Map<String, String>? settings});

  Future<Connection> connect(
    Database database, {
    String? id,
  });

  Future<Connection> connectWithTransferred(
    TransferableDatabase transferableDb, {
    String? id,
  });

  String get version;
}
=== database ===
import 'dart:typed_data';

import 'package:dart_duckdb/src/types/protocol.dart';

/// An opened duckdb database with `dart:ffi`.
abstract class Database {
  /// The native database connection handle from duckdb.
  ///
  /// This returns a pointer towards the opaque duckdb structure as defined
  dynamic get handle;

  /// An unmanaged database reference that can be sent across isolate boundaries.
  /// Disposing the source database, will invalidate this TransferableDatabase.
  ///
  /// Use this to create [Connection]s on separate isolates.
  /// It is recommended to have a single connection per isolate.
  TransferableDatabase get transferable;

  /// Register a file buffer with the database.
  /// This is only for duckdb-wasm.
  Future<void> registerFileBuffer(String name, Uint8List buffer);

  /// Register a file URL with the database.
  /// This is only for duckdb-wasm.
  Future<void> registerFileURL(
    String name,
    String url,
    DuckDBDataProtocol protocol,
    bool directIO,
  );

  /// Register a file handle with the database.
  /// This is only for duckdb-wasm.
  Future<void> registerFileHandle(
    String name,
    dynamic handle,
    DuckDBDataProtocol protocol,
    bool directIO,
  );

  /// Closes this database and releases associated resources.
  Future<void> dispose();
}

/// A database weak reference that can be sent across isolate boundaries.
abstract class TransferableDatabase {}
=== connection ===
import 'package:dart_duckdb/src/api/appender.dart';
import 'package:dart_duckdb/src/api/cancellation_token.dart';
import 'package:dart_duckdb/src/api/prepared_statement.dart';
import 'package:dart_duckdb/src/api/result_set.dart';

/// A DuckDB connection implemented
abstract class Connection {
  /// The native DuckDB connection handle pointer
  dynamic get handle;

  /// The id of the connection
  String? get id;

  /// Executes a query and returns the result set
  /// Can be cancelled via token
  Future<ResultSet> query(String query, {DuckDBCancellationToken? token});

  /// Executes a query without returning results
  /// Can be cancelled,but not interrupted via token
  Future<void> execute(String query, {DuckDBCancellationToken? token});

  /// Creates a prepared statement from a query
  /// Can be cancelled,but not interrupted via token
  Future<PreparedStatement> prepare(
    String query, {
    DuckDBCancellationToken? token,
  });

  /// Creates an appender for inserting data into a table
  Future<Appender> append(String table, String? schema);

  /// Gets the ordered column names for a table
  Future<Iterable<String>> getColumnOrder(String table);

  /// Interrupts the currently running operation
  Future<void> interrupt();

  /// Closes the connection and frees resources
  Future<void> dispose();
}
=== result_set ===
import 'package:dart_duckdb/src/api/column.dart';
import 'package:dart_duckdb/src/api/database_type.dart';

/// An object representing a DuckDB result set
///
/// A DuckDB result set contains the data returned from the database after a
/// successful query.
///
/// A result set is organized into vertical table slices called columns.
abstract class ResultSet {
  /// The number of columns in the result set
  int get columnCount;

  /// The total number of rows in the result set
  int get rowCount;

  /// The names of the columns in the result set
  List<String> get columnNames;

=== duckdb_ffi ===
/// A Dart library for interfacing with the DuckDB database engine.
///
/// This library provides a high-level, idiomatic Dart interface for
/// interacting with DuckDB, an in-process SQL OLAP database management system.
/// It includes support for executing queries, fetching results, and managing
/// transactions.
///
/// To use this library in your code:
/// ```dart
/// import 'package:dart_duckdb/dart_duckdb.dart';
/// ```
library;

import 'dart:ffi';

import 'package:dart_duckdb/src/api/connection.dart';
import 'package:dart_duckdb/src/api/database.dart';
import 'package:dart_duckdb/src/api/duckdb.dart' as api;
import 'package:dart_duckdb/src/ffi/duckdb.g.dart';
import 'package:dart_duckdb/src/ffi/impl/implementation.dart';
import 'package:dart_duckdb/src/ffi/impl/utils.dart';
import 'package:dart_duckdb/src/ffi/load_library.dart' as load;

class BindingsWithLibrary {
  final Bindings bindings;
  final DynamicLibrary library;

  BindingsWithLibrary(this.library) : bindings = Bindings(library);
}

class DuckDB extends api.DuckDB {
  static DuckDB? instance;

  final BindingsWithLibrary _library;
  @override
  Bindings get bindings => _library.bindings;

  DuckDB._internal()
      : _library = BindingsWithLibrary(
          (load.open as load.OpenDynamicLibrary).openDuckDB(),
        );

  // Registers this class as the default instance of [DuckDB]
  static void registerWith() {
    DuckDB.instance = duckdb as DuckDB?;
  }

  /// Open a database, if the database does not exist, it will be created.
  /// Use :memory: to create an in-memory database.
  @override
  Future<Database> open(
    String filename, {
    Map<String, String>? settings,
  }) async {
    return DatabaseImpl.open(_library, dbname: filename, settings: settings)
        as Database;
  }

  /// Create a connection to the database.
  @override
  Future<Connection> connect(Database database, {String? id}) async {
    return ConnectionImpl.connect(database, id: id);
  }

  /// Create a connection to the database with a [TransferableDatabase].
  @override
  Future<Connection> connectWithTransferred(
    TransferableDatabase transferableDatabase, {
    String? id,
  }) async {
    return ConnectionImpl.connectWithTransferred(
      transferableDatabase as TransferableDatabaseImpl,
      id: id,
    );
  }

  /// The version of the duckdb library in used.
  @override
  String get version {
    return bindings.duckdb_library_version().readString();
  }
}

DuckDB? _duckDB;

/// Provides access to `duckdb` functions, such as opening new databases.
api.DuckDB get duckdb {
  return _duckDB ??= DuckDB._internal();
}

/// An object reperesenting a DuckDB prepared statement
///
/// A prepared statement is a parameterized query. The query is prepared with
/// question marks (`?`), dollar symbols (`$1`), or named parameers (`$VVV`)
/// where VVV is alphanumeric, indicating the parameters of the query. Values can then
/// be bound to these parameters, after which the prepared statement can be executed
/// using those parameters. A single query can be prepared once and executed many times.
/// see: https://duckdb.org/docs/api/python/dbapi#named-parameters
///
/// Prepared statements are useful to:
///
///   - Easily supply parameters to functions while avoiding string
///     concatenation/SQL injection attacks.
///   - Speed up queries that will be executed many times with different
///     parameters.
///
/// The following example creates a prepared statement that allows parameters
/// to be bound in two positions within a 'select' statement. The prepared
/// statement is finally executed by calling ``PreparedStatement/execute()``.

///
/// The default behavior can be overridden for a specific OS by using
/// [overrideFor]. To override the behavior on all platforms, use
/// [overrideForAll].
class OpenDynamicLibrary extends OpenLibrary {
  final Map<OperatingSystem, OpenLibraryLoader> _overriddenPlatforms = {};

  OpenDynamicLibrary._();

  /// Returns the current [OperatingSystem] as read from the [Platform] getters.
  OperatingSystem? get os {
    if (Platform.isAndroid) return OperatingSystem.android;
    if (Platform.isIOS) return OperatingSystem.iOS;
    if (Platform.isLinux) return OperatingSystem.linux;
    if (Platform.isMacOS) return OperatingSystem.macOS;
    if (Platform.isWindows) return OperatingSystem.windows;
    return null;
  }

  /// Opens the [DynamicLibrary] from which `duckdb.dart` is going to
  /// [DynamicLibrary.lookup] duckdb's methods that will be used. This method is
  /// meant to be called by `duckdb.dart` only.
  DynamicLibrary openDuckDB() {
    /// If the process already provides has the symbol, we can use it directly.
    /// This is useful when we have loaded the library and are now on an isolate.
    final process = DynamicLibrary.process();
    if (process.providesSymbol('duckdb_open')) {
      return process;
    }

    /// If we are in a test environment, we will load the library from the package.
    if (_isTestEnvironment()) {
      final prefix = '${Directory.current.path}/../duckdb';

      if (_overriddenPlatforms[OperatingSystem.windows] == null) {
        _overriddenPlatforms[OperatingSystem.windows] =
            () => DynamicLibrary.open(
                  '$prefix/windows/Libraries/release/duckdb.dll',
                );
      }

      if (_overriddenPlatforms[OperatingSystem.macOS] == null) {
        _overriddenPlatforms[OperatingSystem.macOS] = () => DynamicLibrary.open(
              '$prefix/macos/Libraries/release/libduckdb.dylib',
            );
      }

      if (_overriddenPlatforms[OperatingSystem.linux] == null) {
        _overriddenPlatforms[OperatingSystem.linux] = () => DynamicLibrary.open(
              '$prefix/linux/Libraries/release/libduckdb.so',
            );
      }
    }

    try {
      final forPlatform = _overriddenPlatforms[os];
      if (forPlatform != null) {
        return forPlatform();
      }
    } catch (error) {
      // ignore
    }

    return _defaultOpen();
  }

  /// Makes `duckdb.dart` use the [open] function when running on the specified
  /// [os]. This can be used to override the loading behavior on some platforms.
  /// To override that behavior on all platforms, consider using
  /// [overrideForAll].
  /// This method must be called before opening any database.
  ///
  /// When using the asynchronous API over isolates, [open] __must be__ a top-
  /// level function or a static method.
  @override
  void overrideFor(OperatingSystem os, String path) {
    _overriddenPlatforms[os] = () => DynamicLibrary.open(path);
  }

  /// Clears all associated open helpers for all platforms.
  @visibleForTesting
  @override
  void reset() {
    _overriddenPlatforms.clear();
  }
}

/// The instance managing different approaches to load the [DynamicLibrary] for
/// duckdb when needed. See the documentation for [OpenDynamicLibrary] to learn
/// how the default opening behavior can be overridden.
final OpenLibrary open = OpenDynamicLibrary._();

}

{
  "command": "cat > lib/db/fleet_db.dart <<'DART'\nimport 'dart:isolate';\n\nimport 'package:dart_duckdb/dart_duckdb.dart';\n\nimport 'schema.dart';\n\n/// The embedded database. One instance per process \u2014 DuckDB takes a file lock,\n/// so extra isolates attach through [transferable] rather than reopening.\n///\n/// This library is deliberately Flutter-free so the DB tests run on the plain\n/// Dart VM. The app supplies the file path (see `lib/main.dart`).\nclass FleetDb {\n  FleetDb._(this._db, this.read);\n\n  final Database _db;\n\n  /// Read-only-by-convention connection for the UI isolate. DuckDB is MVCC,\n  /// so this sees a consistent snapshot while the writer commits.\n  final Connection read;\n\n  /// Pass to [inIsolate] to get a connection on another isolate.\n  TransferableDatabase get transferable => _db.transferable;\n\n  /// Opens [path] (`:memory:` for a throwaway db) and migrates it forward.\n  static Future<FleetDb> open(String path) async {\n    final db = await duckdb.open(path);\n    final conn = await duckdb.connect(db);\n    await _migrate(conn);\n    return FleetDb._(db, conn);\n  }\n\n  /// Runs [job] on its own isolate with its own connection.\n  ///\n  /// [job] must be a top-level or static function: it is sent across an\n  /// isolate boundary, so anything it closes over has to be sendable too.\n  static Future<R> inIsolate<R>(\n    TransferableDatabase transferable,\n    Future<R> Function(Connection) job,\n  ) {\n    return Isolate.run(() async {\n      final conn = await duckdb.connectWithTransferred(transferable);\n      try {\n        return await job(conn);\n      } finally {\n        await conn.dispose();\n      }\n    });\n  }\n\n  Future<void> close() async {\n    await read.dispose();\n    await _db.dispose();\n  }\n}\n\n/// Forward-only migrations, applied in one transaction each so a crash\n/// mid-upgrade leaves the previous version intact rather than a half-schema.\nFuture<void> _migrate(Connection conn) async {\n  await conn.execute(\n    'CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS schema_version (version INTEGER PRIMARY KEY)',\n  );\n  final result = await conn.query(\n    'SELECT coalesce(max(version), 0) FROM schema_version',\n  );\n  final applied = (result.fetchOne()!.first as num).toInt();\n\n  for (var version = applied + 1; version <= migrations.length; version++) {\n    await conn.execute('BEGIN TRANSACTION');\n    try {\n      await conn.execute(migrations[version - 1]);\n      await conn.execute('INSERT INTO schema_version VALUES ($version)');\n      await conn.execute('COMMIT');\n    } catch (_) {\n      await conn.execute('ROLLBACK');\n      rethrow;\n    }\n  }\n}\nDART\ndart format lib/db/fleet_db.dart >/dev/null && dart analyze lib/db 2>&1 | tail -5",
  "description": "Write FleetDb service"
}

async => db = await FleetDb.open(':memory:'));\n  tearDown(() => db.close());\n\n  Future<List<List<Object?>>> rows(String sql) async =>\n      (await db.read.query(sql)).fetchAll();\n\n  test('natural-key PK makes duplicate packets a no-op (\u00a74 step 2)', () async {\n    const packet = \"('v1', 'soc', TIMESTAMP '2026-01-01 10:00:00', 55, now())\";\n    await db.read.execute('INSERT INTO signal_reading VALUES $packet');\n    await db.read\n        .execute('INSERT INTO signal_reading VALUES $packet ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING');\n\n    expect(await rows('SELECT count(*) FROM signal_reading'), [\n      [1]\n    ]);\n  });\n\n  test('conditional ON CONFLICT keeps a late packet from clobbering (\u00a74 step 3)',\n      () async {\n    Future<void> upsert(String ts, num value) => db.read.execute('''\n      INSERT INTO vehicle_signal_latest AS l VALUES\n        ('v1', 'soc', TIMESTAMP '$ts', $value)\n      ON CONFLICT (vehicle_id, signal) DO UPDATE\n        SET value = excluded.value, event_ts = excluded.event_ts\n        WHERE excluded.event_ts > l.event_ts\n    ''');\n\n    await upsert('2026-01-01 10:00:00', 55);\n    await upsert('2026-01-01 10:05:00', 54); // newer, applies\n    await upsert('2026-01-01 09:55:00', 99); // late, must be ignored\n\n    expect(await rows('SELECT event_ts, value FROM vehicle_signal_latest'), [\n      [DateTime.utc(2026, 1, 1, 10, 5), 54.0]\n    ]);\n  });\n\n  test('last_value IGNORE NULLS carries a zone across the hysteresis band (\u00a77.1)',\n      () async {\n    // NULL = fix landed inside the band and has no opinion.\n    const fixes = \"VALUES (1, 'out'), (2, NULL), (3, NULL), (4, 'in'), (5, NULL)\";\n    final carried = await rows('''\n      SELECT t, last_value(zone IGNORE NULLS) OVER (ORDER BY t) AS zone_ff\n      FROM ($fixes) AS f(t, zone) ORDER BY t\n    ''');\n\n    expect(carried.map((r) => r[1]), ['out', 'out', 'out', 'in', 'in']);\n  });\n\n  test('FILTER pivots the latest table in one pass (\u00a75)', () async {\n    await db.read.execute('''\n      INSERT INTO vehicle_signal_latest VALUES\n        ('v1', 'soc',   TIMESTAMP '2026-01-01 10:00:00', 42),\n        ('v1', 'speed', TIMESTAMP '2026-01-01 10:00:30', 0)\n    ''');\n\n    expect(\n      await rows('''\n        SELECT max(value) FILTER (WHERE signal = 'soc')   AS soc,\n               max(value) FILTER (WHERE signal = 'speed') AS speed\n        FROM vehicle_signal_latest GROUP BY vehicle_id\n      '''),\n      [\n        [42.0, 0.0]\n      ],\n    );\n  });\n\n  test('ASOF JOIN reads the odometer as of a trip boundary (\u00a77.2)', () async {\n    await db.read.execute('''\n      INSERT INTO signal_reading VALUES\n        ('v1', 'odometer', TIMESTAMP '2026-01-01 09:00:00', 1000, now()),\n        ('v1', 'odometer', TIMESTAMP '2026-01-01 10:00:00', 1120, now()),\n        ('v1', 'odometer', TIMESTAMP '2026-01-01 11:00:00', 1250, now())\n    ''');\n\n    // The reading in force at 10:30 is the 10:00 one, not the 11:00 one.\n    expect(\n      await rows('''\n        SELECT o.value\n        FROM (SELECT TIMESTAMP '2026-01-01 10:30:00' AS at) b\n        ASOF JOIN signal_reading o ON o.event_ts <= db.read.execute(\"INSERT INTO vehicle VALUES ('v1', 'KA01AB1234', 'eT')\");\n    await db.read.execute('''\n      INSERT INTO signal_reading\n      SELECT v.vehicle_id, 'soc',\n             TIMESTAMP '2026-01-01 00:00:00' + INTERVAL (i * 30) SECOND,\n             100 - (i % 100), now()\n      FROM range(0, 1000) t(i), vehicle v\n    ''');\n\n    expect(await rows('SELECT count(*) FROM signal_reading'), [\n      [1000]\n    ]);\n  });\n}\nDART\ndart format test/db/duckdb_features_test.dart >/dev/null; flutter test test/db/duckdb_features_test.dart 2>&1 | tr '\\r' '\\n' | grep -E dir.create(recursive: true);\n  return p.join(dir.path, 'fleet.duckdb');\n}\n\nclass FleetConsoleApp extends StatelessWidget {\n  const FleetConsoleApp({super.key});\n\n  @override\n  Widget }\n}\n\n/// Placeholder home: opens the database and reports what came back off disk.\n/// Replaced by the fleet list in the next commit \u2014 it exists now so the\n/// local-first path is exercised late final Future<_BootInfo> _info = _load();\n\n  Future<_BootInfo> _load() async {\n    final path = await databasePath();\n    final db = await FleetDb.open(path);\n    final result = await db.read.query('''\n      SELECT (SELECT max(version) FROM schema_version),\n             (SELECT count(*) FROM vehicle),\n             (SELECT count(*) FROM signal_reading)\n    ''');\n    final row = result.fetchOne()!;\n    return _BootInfo(\n      path: path,\n      schemaVersion: (row[0] as num).toInt(),\n      vehicles: (row[1] as num).toInt(),\n      readings: (row[2] as num).toInt(),\n    );\n  }\n\n  @override\n  Widget build(BuildContext context) {\n    return Scaffold(\n      appBar: AppBar(title: const Text('Fleet Console')),\n      body: FutureBuilder<_BootInfo>(\n        future:        if (info == null) {\n            return const Center(child: CircularProgressIndicator());\n          }\n          return ListView(\n            children: [\n              ListTile(title: const Text('Database'), subtitle: Text(info.path)),\n              ListTile(\n                title: const Text('Schema version'),\n                trailing: Text('${info.schemaVersion}'),\n              ),\n              ListTile(\n                title: const Text('Vehicles'),\n                trailing: Text('${info.vehicles}'),\n              ),\n              ListTile(\n                title: ### Claude — 2026-09-12T08:04:02.473Z
Skeleton is up and verified on both target platforms.

```
lib/db/schema.dart      full §2 DDL + forward-only migration list
lib/db/fleet_db.dart    open · migrate · read connection · inIsolate()
lib/main.dart           boot screen reading counts back off disk
test/duckdb_support.dart
test/db/fleet_db_test.dart          3 tests
test/db/duckdb_features_test.dart   6 tests
tool/fetch_duckdb_lib.sh
```

`lib/db/` is Flutter-free, so DB tests run on the plain VM. Schema is a Dart string, not a bundled `.sql` — the writer isolate has no `rootBundle`, and migrations run there.

**Verified, not assumed:**
- 9/9 tests pass; `flutter build macos` and `flutter build apk` both green, `libduckdb.so` in the APK for arm64-v8a and armeabi-v7a; the macOS app creates and reopens `fleet.duckdb` in Application Support.
- `test/db/fleet_db_test.dart` pins the three things §1 rests on: a writer on a spawned isolate, a reader on the main one seeing its commit, and the data still there after close-and-reopen.
- `test/db/duckdb_features_test.dart` pins every SQL feature ARCHITECTURE.md leans on — conditional `ON CONFLICT … WHERE excluded.event_ts > l.event_ts`, `last_value(… IGNORE NULLS)`, `FILTER`, `ASOF JOIN`, `range()`, PK dedupe. A DuckDB bump that breaks one now fails here instead of in the app. It already earned its keep: my doc had `FILTER (signal = …)`, the real syntax is `FILTER (WHERE …)` — doc corrected.

**Two things worth knowing:**

`dart_duckdb` is pinned to **1.2.2 exactly**, not `^1.2.0`. Packages 1.4.1–1.4.4 point their Android Gradle task at release tags with no Android binaries — `v1.4.1` and `v1.4.4` both 404, so the APK cannot build. 1.2.2 pulls from `v1.2.0`, which exists. That's almost certainly why the brief names `^1.2.0`. Engine is DuckDB 1.2.1, and `tool/fetch_duckdb_lib.sh` fetches that same version so host tests and device run identical SQL.

`flutter test` needs `tool/fetch_duckdb_lib.sh` run once — the package's own test-mode fallback points at paths inside its monorepo, which don't exist in a consumer app. One line in the README.

skipped: no ingest, no features, no writer-isolate loop — `inIsolate()` exists only because it de-risks the transferable-handle assumption. Everything uncommitted for you to review; `git init` done, nothing staged.

--- appender api ---
/// An open DuckDB database using `dart:ffi`.
abstract class Appender {
  /// The native appender handle from DuckDB.
  ///
  /// This returns a pointer to the opaque DuckDB structure as defined in the FFI.
  dynamic get handle;

  /// Closes the appender and releases associated resources.
  void dispose();

  /// Appends a value for the current row of the specified type.
  ///
  /// Appends are done in a row-wise format. For each column in a row,
  /// an `append` call should be made. After appending all columns,
  /// `endRow` should be called to complete the row.
  ///
  /// @param value: The value to append.
  /// @throws DuckDBException if a value of this type was not expected
  /// in the appender's current state.
  void append(Object? value);

  /// Marks the end of the current row.
  ///
  /// After all columns for a row have been appended, `endRow` must be
  /// called to signal that the row is complete and ready to be added
  /// to the database.
  ///
  /// @throws DuckDBException if the row could not be completed
  /// in its current state.
  void endRow();

  /// Flushes pending rows to the database.
  ///
  /// To enhance performance, the appender writes rows to the database in
  /// batches. Use `flush` to immediately write any pending rows.
  ///
  /// @throws DuckDBException if the pending rows failed to be written
  /// to the database.
  void flush();
}

'' : ' ($cause)'}';\n}\n\n/// The packet source could not produce packets.\nclass PacketSourceException implements Exception {\n  const PacketSourceException(this.message);\n\n  final String message;\n\n  @override\n  String toString() => 'PacketSourceException: $message';\n}\nDART\n\ncat > lib/core/utils/result.dart <<'DART'\nimport '../error/failures.dart';\n\n/// A success-or-failure wrapper for use-case return values.\n///\n/// Clean-architecture examples usually reach for `dartz`'s `Either` here. Dart\n/// 3 sealed classes plus exhaustive `switch` give the same guarantee \u2014 you\n/// cannot read the value without handling the failure \u2014 without adding a\n/// functional-programming dependency for one type.\nsealed class Result<T> {\n  const Result();\n\n  /// True when this holds a value.\n  bool get isOk => this is Ok<T>;\n\n  /// The value, or null when this is a failure. Streams carry no `Result`\n/// because a broken source terminates the stream with an error instead.\nabstract class StreamUseCase<Type, Params> {\n  /// Opens the stream.\n  Stream<Type> call(Params params);\n}\n\n/// Parameter object for use cases that take no arguments.\nclass NoParams {\n  const NoParams();\n}\nDART\n\ncat > lib/core/utils/clock.dart <<'DART'\n/// Wall-clock access, injected rather than called statically.\n///\n/// Freshness, the OFFLINE rule and alert evaluation all compare event time\n/// against \"now\". {
  "command": "cd /Users/durgeshparekh/Documents/GitHub/byte_beam_project\ncat > lib/features/telemetry_ingest/data/datasources/telemetry_writer_isolate.dart <<'DART'\nimport 'dart:isolate';\n\nimport 'package:dart_duckdb/dart_duckdb.dart';\n\nimport '../../../../core/error/exceptions.dart';\nimport '../models/telemetry_packet_model.dart';\n\n/// The single writer.\n///\n/// DuckDB allows one writer at a time, so every mutation in the app funnels\n/// through this long-lived isolate. It is spawned once and kept: spawning a\n/// fresh isolate per batch (as `Isolate.run` would) costs more than the write.\n///\n/// The UI isolate keeps its own read connection and sees a consistent MVCC\n/// snapshot while this one commits, which is what stops a 3-hour backlog dump\n/// from freezing the fleet list.\nclass TelemetryWriter {\n  TelemetryWriter._(this._isolate, this._commands);\n\n  final Isolate _isolate;\n  final SendPort _commands;\n\n  /// Starts the writer and waits until it has a connection and its staging\n  /// tables in place. Returns only once it is ready to accept batches.\n  static Future<TelemetryWriter> spawn(TransferableDatabase transferable) async {\n    final handshake = ReceivePort();\n    final isolate = await Isolate.spawn(\n      _writerMain,\n      _WriterBoot(handshake.sendPort, transferable),\n      debugName: 'telemetry-writer',\n    );\n\n    final ready = await handshake.first;\n    handshake.close();\n    if (ready is _WriterFailed) {\n      isolate.kill(priority: Isolate.immediate);\n      throw LocalDatabaseException('writer isolate failed to start', ready.error);\n    }\n    return TelemetryWriter._(isolate, ready as SendPort);\n  }\n\n  /// Applies one batch durably and returns what the database actually did.\n  ///\n  /// A fresh reply port per request keeps this free of request-id bookkeeping;\n  /// the allocation is noise next to the write itself.\n  Future<IngestReceiptModel> applyBatch(List<TelemetryPacketModel> batch) async {\n    final reply = ReceivePort();\n    _commands.send(_WriteRequest(reply.sendPort, batch));\n    final response = await reply.first as _WriteResponse;\n    reply.close();\n\n    final error = response.error;\n    if (error != null) throw LocalDatabaseException('ingest batch failed', error);\n    return response.receipt!;\n  }\n\n  /// Closes the writer's connection and stops the isolate.\n  Future<void> dispose() async {\n    final reply = ReceivePort();\n    _commands.send(_Shutdown(reply.sendPort));\n    await reply.first.timeout(\n      const Duration(seconds: 5),\n      onTimeout: () => _isolate.kill(priority: Isolate.immediate),\n    );\n    reply.close();\n  }\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- isolate --\n\n/// Isolate entry point. Top-level because `Isolate.spawn` cannot take a\n/// closure that captures anything unsendable.\nFuture<void> _writerMain(_WriterBoot boot) async {\n  final Connection conn;\n  try {\n    conn = await duckdb.connectWithTransferred(boot.transferable);\n    await _createStagingTables(conn);\n  } catch (error) {\n    boot.reply.send(_WriterFailed(error.toString()));\n    return;\n  }\n\n  final commands = ReceivePort();\n  boot.reply.send(commands.sendPort);\n\n  await for (final message in commands) {\n    if (message is _Shutdown) {\n      await conn.dispose();\n      commands.close();\n      message.reply.send(null);\n      return;\n    }\n    final request = message as _WriteRequest;\n    try {\n      request.reply.send(_WriteResponse(receipt: await _applyBatch(conn, request.batch)));\n    } catch (error) {\n      request.reply.send(_WriteResponse(error: error.toString()));\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n/// Staging tables the appender writes into.\n///\n/// Batches land here first so the real inserts can be set-based: one\n/// `INSERT \u2026 SELECT` per target table instead of a statement per row, and the\n/// intra-batch de-duplication becomes a `DISTINCT ON` rather than a Dart loop.\n/// They are TEMP, so they belong to this connection and never touch the file.\nFuture<void> _createStagingTables(Connection conn) async {\n  await conn.execute('''\n    CREATE TEMP TABLE staging_signal (\n      vehicle_id TEXT, signal TEXT, event_ts TIMESTAMP, value DOUBLE\n    );\n    CREATE TEMP TABLE staging_location (\n      vehicle_id TEXT, event_ts TIMESTAMP, lat DOUBLE, lon DOUBLE, accuracy_m DOUBLE\n    );\n  ''');\n}\n\n/// One batch, end to end. Mirrors ARCHITECTURE.md \u00a74 step for step.\nFuture<IngestReceiptModel> _applyBatch(\n  Connection conn,\n  List<TelemetryPacketModel> batch,\n) async {\n  final stopwatch = Stopwatch()..start();\n\n  await _stage(conn, batch);\n\n  // Transaction 1 \u2014 the log and the latest-value table.\n  await conn.execute('BEGIN TRANSACTION');\n  final int signalsApplied;\n  final int locationsApplied;\n  try {\n    signalsApplied = await _appendSignalLog(conn);\n    locationsApplied = await _appendLocationLog(conn);\n    await _advanceLatest(conn);\n    await conn.execute('COMMIT');\n  } catch (_) {\n    await conn.execute('ROLLBACK');\n    rethrow;\n  }\n\n  // Transaction 2 \u2014 derivation position. Split from the first so a long\n  // recompute never holds a write lock across the whole batch, and so the\n  // watermark can only ever lag the log, never lead it (\u00a73.4).\n  await conn.execute('BEGIN TRANSACTION');\n  final int lateVehicles;\n  try {\n    lateVehicles = await _countLateVehicles(conn);\n    // Derivation (alerts, geofence transitions, trips) slots in here, before\n    // the watermark moves. Until it exists the watermark simply records how\n    // far the log has been read.\n    await _advanceWatermark(conn);\n    await conn.execute('COMMIT');\n  } catch (_) {\n    await conn.execute('ROLLBACK');\n    rethrow;\n  }\n\n  final offered = await _stagedCounts(conn);\n  return IngestReceiptModel(\n    packets: batch.length,\n    signalRowsOffered: offered.$1,\n    signalRowsApplied: signalsApplied,\n    locationRowsOffered: offered.$2,\n    locationRowsApplied: locationsApplied,\n    lateVehicles: lateVehicles,\n    duration: stopwatch.elapsed,\n  );\n}\n\n/// Empties the staging tables and bulk-appends this batch into them.\n///\n/// The appender is DuckDB's row-wise bulk path \u2014 no SQL text, no parameter\n/// binding, no string escaping of vehicle ids.\nFuture<void> _stage(Connection conn, List<TelemetryPacketModel> batch) async {\n  await conn.execute('DELETE FROM staging_signal; DELETE FROM staging_location;');\n\n  final signals = await conn.append('staging_signal', 'temp.main');\n  final locations = await conn.append('staging_location', 'temp.main');\n  try {\n    for (final packet in batch) {\n      for (final row in packet.toSignalRows()) {\n        for (final value in row) {\n          signals.append(value);\n        }\n        signals.endRow();\n      }\n      final location = packet.toLocationRow();\n      if (location != null) {\n        for (final value in location) {\n          locations.append(value);\n        }\n        locations.endRow();\n      }\n    }\n    signals.flush();\n    locations.flush();\n  } finally {\n    signals.dispose();\n    locations.dispose();\n  }\n}\n\n/// Appends the batch to the event log.\n///\n/// `DISTINCT ON` collapses duplicates *within* the batch; `ON CONFLICT DO\n/// NOTHING` against the natural primary key collapses duplicates against\n/// everything already on disk. Re-delivering a packet is therefore a no-op\n/// enforced by the database, not by application code.\nFuture<int> _appendSignalLog(Connection conn) async {\n  return _countOf(conn, '''\n    INSERT INTO signal_reading\n    SELECT vehicle_id, signal, event_ts, value, CAST(now() AS TIMESTAMP)\n    FROM (\n      SELECT DISTINCT ON (vehicle_id, signal, event_ts) *\n      FROM staging_signal\n      ORDER BY vehicle_id, signal, event_ts\n    )\n    ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING\n  ''');\n}\n\n/// Same contract as [_appendSignalLog], for position reports.\nFuture<int> _appendLocationLog(Connection conn) async {\n  return _countOf(conn, '''\n    INSERT INTO location_fix\n    SELECT vehicle_id, event_ts, lat, lon, accuracy_m, CAST(now() AS TIMESTAMP)\n    FROM (\n      SELECT DISTINCT ON (vehicle_id, event_ts) *\n      FROM staging_location\n      ORDER BY vehicle_id, event_ts\n    )\n    ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING\n  ''');\n}\n\n/// Moves the latest-value table forward.\n///\n/// The `WHERE excluded.event_ts > l.event_ts` guard is the entire late-packet\n/// story for the read path: a packet that arrives late but measures an older\n/// moment updates the log and leaves the current value alone.\nFuture<void> _advanceLatest(Connection conn) async {\n  await conn.execute('''\n    INSERT INTO vehicle_signal_latest AS l\n    SELECT vehicle_id, signal, event_ts, value\n    FROM (\n      SELECT DISTINCT ON (vehicle_id, signal) *\n      FROM staging_signal\n      ORDER BY vehicle_id, signal, event_ts DESC\n    )\n    ON CONFLICT (vehicle_id, signal) DO UPDATE\n      SET value = excluded.value, event_ts = excluded.event_ts\n      WHERE excluded.event_ts > l.event_ts\n  ''');\n}\n\n/// Counts vehicles whose batch reaches back behind where derivation already\n/// ran. These are the replays that geofence transitions and trips will need.\nFuture<int> _countLateVehicles(Connection conn) async {\n  final result = await conn.query('''\n    SELECT count(*) FROM (\n      SELECT s.vehicle_id\n      FROM staging_signal s JOIN ingest_watermark w USING (vehicle_id)\n      WHERE s.event_ts < w.processed_through\n      UNION\n      SELECT l.vehicle_id\n      FROM staging_location l JOIN ingest_watermark w USING (vehicle_id)\n      WHERE l.event_ts < w.processed_through\n    )\n  ''');\n  return (result.fetchOne()!.first as num).toInt();\n}\n\n/// Records how far each vehicle's log has been processed.\n///\n/// Guarded the same way as the latest-value table: a late batch must not drag\n/// a vehicle's watermark backwards.\nFuture<void> _advanceWatermark(Connection conn) async {\n  await conn.execute('''\n    INSERT INTO ingest_watermark AS w\n    SELECT vehicle_id, max(event_ts) FROM (\n      SELECT vehicle_id, event_ts FROM staging_signal\n      UNION ALL\n      SELECT vehicle_id, event_ts FROM staging_location\n    ) GROUP BY vehicle_id\n    ON CONFLICT (vehicle_id) DO UPDATE\n      SET processed_through = excluded.processed_through\n      WHERE excluded.processed_through > w.processed_through\n  ''');\n}\n\n/// Staged row counts after intra-batch de-duplication \u2014 the denominator the\n/// receipt's duplicate count is measured against.\nFuture<(int, int)> _stagedCounts(Connection conn) async {\n  final result = await conn.query('''\n    SELECT (SELECT count(*) FROM (SELECT DISTINCT vehicle_id, signal, event_ts FROM staging_signal)),\n           (SELECT count(*) FROM (SELECT DISTINCT vehicle_id, event_ts FROM staging_location))\n  ''');\n  final row = result.fetchOne()!;\n  return ((row[0] as num).toInt(), (row[1] as num).toInt());\n}\n\n/// Runs a mutating statement and returns the row count DuckDB reports.\nFuture<int> _countOf(Connection conn, String sql) async {\n  final result = await conn.query(sql);\n  final row = result.fetchOne();\n  return row == null ? 0 : (row.first as num).toInt();\n}\n\n// --------------------------------------------------------------- messages --\n\n/// Startup payload: where to reply, and the database handle to attach to.\nclass _WriterBoot {\n  const _WriterBoot(this.reply, this.transferable);\n\n  final SendPort reply;\n  final TransferableDatabase transferable;\n}\n\n/// Sent back when the writer could not attach to the database.\nclass _WriterFailed {\n  const _WriterFailed(this.error);\n\n  final String error;\n}\n\n/// One batch to write, and where to send the receipt.\nclass _WriteRequest {\n  const _WriteRequest(this.reply, this.batch);\n\n  final SendPort reply;\n  final List<TelemetryPacketModel> batch;\n}\n\n/// Either a receipt or the error string that replaced it.\nclass _WriteResponse {\n  const _WriteResponse({this.receipt, this.error});\n\n  final IngestReceiptModel? receipt;\n  final String? error;\n}\n\n/// Asks the writer to close its connection and stop.\nclass _Shutdown {\n  const _Shutdown(this.reply);\n\n  final SendPort reply;\n}\nDART\ndart format lib/features >/dev/null && dart analyze lib/features 2>&1 | tail -5",
  "description": "Write the writer isolate"
}
