It models vehicles well enough that the fleet list, the alert\n/// thresholds and the geofence logic have realistic material to chew on. And\n/// it misbehaves on purpose \u2014 duplicating, delaying, dropping and buffering\n/// packets \u2014 because the pipeline is built for a link that does all four and\n/// a well-behaved source would never exercise that.\n///\n/// Every random decision comes from one seeded generator, so a run is\n/// reproducible: a failing test replays exactly.\nclass SimulatedPacketSource implements TelemetryPacketSource {\n  SimulatedPacketSource({\n    required Clock clock,\n    this.config = const SimulatorConfig(),\n  })  : _clock = clock,\n        _random = Random(config.seed) {\n    _vehicles = List.generate(config.vehicleCount, _buildVehicle);\n  }\n\n  final SimulatorConfig config;\n  final Clock _clock;\n  final Random _random;\n\n  late final List<_SimulatedVehicle> _vehicles;\n\n  /// Packets deliberately held back: late arrivals and duplicates waiting for\n  /// their release tick. They keep their original event time.\n  final List<_DelayedPacket> _delayed = [];\n\n  StreamController<List<TelemetryPacket>>? _controller;\n  Timer? _timer;\n  int _tickCount = 0;\n\n  @override\n  List<FleetVehicle> get fleet => [\n        for (final vehicle in _vehicles)\n          FleetVehicle(\n            vehicleId: vehicle.id,\n            regNo: vehicle.regNo,\n            model: vehicle.model,\n          ),\n      ];\n\n  /// Starts the timer and emits a batch per configured tick.\n  ///\n  /// Single-subscription: the repository is the only listener, and the\n  /// controller pauses the timer when nobody is listening.\n  @override\n  Stream<List<TelemetryPacket>> stream() {\n    final controller = _controller ??= StreamController<List<TelemetryPacket>>(\n      onCancel: dispose,\n    );\n    _timer ??= Timer.periodic(config.tick, (_) {\n      if (controller.isClosed) return;\n      final batch = generateTick();\n      if (batch.isNotEmpty) controller.add(batch);\n    });\n    return controller.stream;\n  }\n\n  @override\n  Future<void> dispose() async {\n    _timer?.cancel();\n    _timer = null;\n    await _controller?.close();\n    _controller = null;\n  }\n\n  /// Produces exactly one tick's worth of arrivals.\n  ///\n  /// Public and synchronous so tests can drive the simulator without real\n  /// timers \u2014 the fault injection is the part worth asserting on, and waiting\n  /// half a second per tick to observe it would be silly.\n  List<TelemetryPacket> generateTick() {\n    _tickCount++;\n    final now = _clock.nowUtc();\n    final arrivals = <TelemetryPacket>[];\n\n    for (final vehicle in _vehicles) {\n      _advance(vehicle);\n      final packet = _emit(vehicle, now);\n      if (packet.isEmpty) continue;\n      _route(vehicle, packet, arrivals);\n    }\n\n    _releaseDue(arrivals);\n\n    // Arrival order is not emission order on a real link. Shuffling with the\n    // seeded generator keeps that true and still reproducible.\n    arrivals.shuffle(_random);\n    return arrivals;\n  }\n\n  // ------------------------------------------------------------- routing --\n\n  /// Decides what the network does to one freshly measured packet: drop it,\n  /// buffer it behind a dead link, deliver it now, deliver it late, and/or\n  /// deliver it twice.\n  void _route(\n    _SimulatedVehicle vehicle,\n    TelemetryPacket packet,\n    List<TelemetryPacket> arrivals,\n  ) {\n    // A vehicle parked in a basement keeps measuring and keeps nothing\n    // flowing. The backlog lands in one burst when the link returns.\n    if (vehicle.darkTicks > 0) {\n      vehicle.buffer.add(packet);\n      vehicle.darkTicks--;\n      if (vehicle.darkTicks == 0) {\n        arrivals.addAll(vehicle.buffer);\n        vehicle.buffer.clear();\n      }\n      return;\n    }\n    if (_random.nextDouble() < config.backlogChance) {\n      vehicle.darkTicks = config.backlogTicks;\n      vehicle.buffer.add(packet);\n      return;\n    }\n\n    if (_random.nextDouble() < config.dropRate) return;\n\n    if (_random.nextDouble() < config.lateRate) {\n      // Held back, but still stamped with when it was measured \u2014 this is the\n      // out-of-order case, not a fresh reading.\n      _delayed.add(_DelayedPacket(packet, _tickCount + 1 + _random.nextInt(4)));\n    } else {\n      arrivals.add(packet);\n    }\n\n    // A retransmit: byte-identical, same event time, so the natural key is\n    // what has to reject it. Sent a tick or two later to exercise the\n    // cross-batch case rather than the easy within-batch one.\n    if (_random.nextDouble() < config.duplicateRate) {\n      _delayed.add(_DelayedPacket(packet, _tickCount + 1 + _random.nextInt(3)));\n    }\n  }\n\n  /// Moves delayed packets into this tick's arrivals once their time is up.\n  void _releaseDue(List<TelemetryPacket> arrivals) {\n    _delayed.removeWhere((delayed) {\n      if (delayed.releaseTick > _tickCount) return false;\n      arrivals.add(delayed.packet);\n      return true;\n    });\n  }\n\n  // ------------------------------------------------------------- physics --\n\n  /// Advances one vehicle by one tick.\n  ///\n  /// Crude on purpose: the pipeline does not care whether the curves are\n  /// right, only that values move plausibly, stay in range, and occasionally\n  /// cross a threshold.\n  void _advance(_SimulatedVehicle vehicle) {\n    final hours = config.tick.inMilliseconds / 3600000.0;\n\n    // Ignition flips rarely, so vehicles hold a state long enough to be worth\n    // looking at in the fleet list.\n    if (_random.nextDouble() < 0.02) vehicle.ignition = !vehicle.ignition;\n\n    if (!vehicle.ignition) {\n      vehicle.speed = 0;\n      vehicle.batteryTemp = _towards(vehicle.batteryTemp, 28, 0.4);\n      return;\n    }\n\n    // Speed wanders and is occasionally zero \u2014 that is the IDLE case, ignition\n    // on and not moving.\n    vehicle.speed = _random.nextDouble() < 0.25\n        ? 0\n        : (vehicle.speed + (_random.nextDouble() - 0.45) * 18).clamp(0, 80);\n\n    final km = vehicle.speed * hours;\n    vehicle.odometer += km;\n    vehicle.soc = (vehicle.soc - km * vehicle.drainPerKm).clamp(0, 100);\n\n    // Charging: a flat truck plugs in rather than sitting at 0% forever.\n    if (vehicle.soc <= 1) vehicle.soc = 100;\n\n    vehicle.batteryTemp = _towards(\n      vehicle.batteryTemp,\n      28 + vehicle.speed * 0.22 + vehicle.tempBias,\n      0.35,\n    );\n\n    // Straight-line drift with an occasional turn. Good enough to cross a\n    // geofence boundary, which is all the later features need.\n    if (_random.nextDouble() < 0.15) {\n      vehicle.heading = (vehicle.heading + (_random.nextDouble() - 0.5) * 2) % (2 * pi);\n    }\n    vehicle.lat += km / 111.0 * cos(vehicle.heading);\n    vehicle.lon += km / (111.0 * cos(vehicle.lat * pi / 180)) * sin(vehicle.heading);\n  }\n\n  /// Exponential approach, so temperatures ramp instead of teleporting.\n  double _towards(double current, double target, double rate) =>\n      current + (target - current) * rate;\n\n  // ------------------------------------------------------------ emission --\n\n  /// Builds the packet a vehicle emits this tick.\n  ///\n  /// Signals report at different cadences, so a packet carries a *subset* \u2014\n  /// which is exactly why freshness is tracked per signal and not per vehicle.\n  TelemetryPacket _emit(_SimulatedVehicle vehicle, DateTime now) {\n    final signals = <String, double>{\n      'speed': vehicle.speed,\n      'ignition': vehicle.ignition ? 1 : 0,\n    };\n    if (_tickCount % 2 == 0) {\n      signals['soc'] = double.parse(vehicle.soc.toStringAsFixed(1));\n      signals['range_km'] = double.parse((vehicle.soc * 3.2).toStringAsFixed(1));\n    }\n    if (_tickCount % 3 == 0) {\n      signals['battery_temp'] =\n          double.parse(vehicle.batteryTemp.toStringAsFixed(1));\n    }\n    if (_tickCount % 4 == 0) {\n      signals['odometer'] = double.parse(vehicle.odometer.toStringAsFixed(2));\n    }\n\n    // Position reports while driving, sparsely while parked. Accuracy is\n    // usually good and occasionally terrible \u2014 the bad fixes are what the\n    // geofence accuracy gate exists to throw away.\n    final reportsFix = vehicle.ignition || _tickCount % 6 == 0;\n    final fix = reportsFix\n        ? GeoFix(\n            lat: vehicle.lat,\n            lon: vehicle.lon,\n            accuracyM: _random.nextDouble() < 0.08\n                ? 60 + _random.nextDouble() * 180\n                : 4 + _random.nextDouble() * 12,\n          )\n        : null;\n\n    return TelemetryPacket(\n      vehicleId: vehicle.id,\n      eventTs: now,\n      signals: signals,\n      location: fix,\n    );\n  }\n\n  // --------------------------------------------------------------- fleet --\n\n  /// Builds one truck. Deterministic from its index, so the same seed always\n  /// produces the same fleet with the same problem children.\n  _SimulatedVehicle _buildVehicle(int index) {\n    const models = ['eT 1000', 'eT 1500', 'eHaul 400'];\n    const letters = 'ABCDEFGHJKLMNPQRSTUVWXYZ';\n    final regNo = 'KA01'\n        '${letters[index % letters.length]}${letters[(index ~/ 24) % letters.length]}'\n        '${(1000 + index * 7 % 9000)}';\n\n    return _SimulatedVehicle(\n      id: 'v${index.toString().padLeft(3, '0')}',\n      regNo: regNo,\n      model: models[index % models.length],\n      // Every seventh truck starts near the low-battery threshold and every\n      // eleventh runs hot, so the alert rules have something to fire on\n      // without waiting for a lucky random walk.\n      soc: index % 7 == 0 ? 14 + _random.nextDouble() * 10 : 35 + _random.nextDouble() * 60,\n      drainPerKm: index % 7 == 0 ? 0.55 : 0.22,\n      tempBias: index % 11 == 0 ? 16 : 0,\n      batteryTemp: 30 + _random.nextDouble() * 5,\n      odometer: 12000 + _random.nextDouble() * 90000,\n      lat: 12.90 + _random.nextDouble() * 0.14,\n      lon: 77.52 + _random.nextDouble() * 0.16,\n      heading: _random.nextDouble() * 2 * pi,\n      ignition: _random.nextBool(),\n    );\n  }\n}\n\n/// Mutable state for one simulated truck.\nclass _SimulatedVehicle {\n  _SimulatedVehicle({\n    required this.id,\n    required this.regNo,\n    required this.model,\n    required this.soc,\n    required this.drainPerKm,\n    required this.tempBias,\n    required this.batteryTemp,\n    required this.odometer,\n    required this.lat,\n    required this.lon,\n    required this.heading,\n    required this.ignition,\n  });\n\n  final String id;\n  final String regNo;\n  final String model;\n\n  /// How fast this truck eats charge, in % per km.\n  final double drainPerKm;\n\n  /// Degrees added to this truck's steady-state battery temperature.\n  final double tempBias;\n\n  double soc;\n  double batteryTemp;\n  double odometer;\n  double speed = 0;\n  double lat;\n  double lon;\n  double heading;\n  bool ignition;\n\n  /// Ticks left before the link comes back.\n  int darkTicks = 0;\n\n  /// Packets measured while the link was down.\n  final List<TelemetryPacket> buffer = [];\n}\n\n/// A packet the network is sitting on until [releaseTick].\nclass _DelayedPacket {\n  const _DelayedPacket(this.packet, this.releaseTick);\n\n  final TelemetryPacket packet;\n  final int releaseTick;\n}\nDART\ndart format lib/features >/dev/null && dart analyze lib/features 2>&1 | tail -5",
  "description": "Write the packet simulator"
}

Swapping the simulator for a real feed changes nothing here.\nclass IngestController extends GetxController {\n  IngestController({\n    required SeedFleet seedFleet,\n    required ObserveTelemetry observeTelemetry,\n    required IngestPacketBatch ingestPacketBatch,\n    required GetIngestSnapshot getIngestSnapshot,\n    required List<FleetVehicle> roster,\n  })  : _seedFleet = seedFleet,\n        _observeTelemetry = observeTelemetry,\n        _ingestPacketBatch = ingestPacketBatch,\n        _getIngestSnapshot = getIngestSnapshot,\n        _roster = roster;\n\n  final SeedFleet _seedFleet;\n  final ObserveTelemetry _observeTelemetry;\n  final IngestPacketBatch _ingestPacketBatch;\n  final GetIngestSnapshot _getIngestSnapshot;\n  final List<FleetVehicle> _roster;\n\n  StreamSubscription<List<TelemetryPacket>>? _subscription;\n\n  /// True while the feed is connected.\n  final isRunning = false.obs;\n\n  /// Last error message, or empty. These are *session* counters and\n  /// deliberately separate from [snapshot], which is what is actually on disk.\n  final packetsReceived = 0.obs;\n  final rowsApplied = 0.obs;\n  final duplicatesRejected = 0.obs;\n  final lateVehicles = 0.obs;\n  final batchesWritten = 0.obs;\n\n  /// Duration of the most recent batch, in milliseconds.\n  final lastBatchMs = 0.obs;\n\n  /// What a fresh query against the database returns. Opening DuckDB and spawning the writer isolate are\n/// both async, and a screen that builds before its writer exists would have to\n/// carry a \"not ready yet\" state that never means anything useful.\nFuture<void> main() async {\n  WidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized();\n\n  final db = await FleetDb.open(await databasePath());\n  Get.put<FleetDb>(db, permanent: true);\n\n  await IngestBinding(db: db).dependenciesAsync();\n\n  runApp(const FleetConsoleApp());\n}\n\n/// Where the database lives. Application support, not documents or cache:\n/// it is app-private state the OS must not evict.\nFuture<String> databasePath() async {\n  final dir = await getApplicationSupportDirectory();\n  await dir.create(recursive: true);\n  return p.join(dir.path, 'fleet.duckdb');\n}\n\n/// Root widget. `GetMaterialApp` rather than `MaterialApp` so routes and\n/// bindings go through GetX.\nclass FleetConsoleApp extends StatelessWidget {\n  const FleetConsoleApp({super.key});\n\n  @override\n  Widget build(BuildContext context) {\n    return GetMaterialApp(\n      title: 'Fleet Console',\n      debugShowCheckedModeBanner: false,\n      theme: ThemeData(colorSchemeSeed: Colors.teal, useMaterial3: true),\n      home: const IngestPage(),\n    );\n  }\n}\nDART\ndart format lib >/dev/null && flutter analyze 2>&1 | tail -4",

keys.toSet().length, greaterThan(0),\n        reason: 'no duplicates were injected, so dedupe is untested');\n  });\n\n  test('it delivers packets out of order', () {\n    final feed = arrivals(newSource(newClock()), newClock(), 60);\n\n    // A late arrival is one measured before something that already arrived.\n    var newestSeen = DateTime.utc(2000);\n    var lateArrivals = 0;\n    for (final (_, packet) in feed) {\n      if (packet.eventTs.isBefore(newestSeen)) lateArrivals++;\n      if (packet.eventTs.isAfter(newestSeen)) newestSeen = packet.eventTs;\n    }\n\n    expect(lateArrivals, greaterThan(0),\n        reason: 'no out-of-order arrivals, so the late path is untested');\n  });\n\n  test('it drops packets and buffers backlogs', () {\n    final source = () {\n    final feed = drive(10);\n    final distinctTimes = feed.map((a) => a.$2.eventTs).toSet();\n\n    expect(distinctTimes.length, greaterThan(1),\n        reason: 'a frozen clock would make expect(keys.length - keys.toSet().length, greaterThan(0),\n        reason: 'no duplicates injected, so the dedupe path is untested');\n  });\n\n  test('it delivers packets out of order', () {\n    var (packet.eventTs.isAfter(newestSeen)) newestSeen = packet.eventTs;\n    }\n\n    expect(lateArrivals, greaterThan(0),\n        reason: 'no out-of-order arrivals, so the late path is untested');\n ### User — 2026-09-12T09:22:05.851Z
**result**
```
## 1. Layout

```
lib/
  db/          schema.sql · migrations · DuckDbService (isolate, connections)
  ingest/      PacketSource (simulator) · IngestPipeline · Backfill
  derive/      SQL derivation: latest · alerts · transitions · trips
  query/       read-side queries returning plain Dart records
  features/    fleet · vehicle · alerts · geofences · trips  (Riverpod + widgets)
tool/
  bench.dart           cold start / p50 / p95 / memory harness
  export_ai_logs.sh    Claude session jsonl -> ai-logs/*.md
ai-logs/       uncurated AI conversation logs (deliverable 3)
```

No repository interfaces, no DAO layer, no abstract `TelemetryService`. One implementation each; a swap is a constructor argument, not an abstraction. Query functions return records/freezed models straight from SQL.

### Isolates

DuckDB is a single-writer, MVCC embedded engine. One process, so multiple connections against one instance are fine — readers get a snapshot while the writer commits.

- **Writer isolate** — owns ingest, derivation, backfill. Everything that mutates runs here, serialised, in transactions.
- **UI isolate** — read-only connection, queries on demand and on a *debounced* change ping (250 ms). A 3-hour backlog dump must not repaint the fleet list 500 times.

`dart_duckdb` exposes a transferable database handle for exactly this. Ingest batches are bounded (≤ 5k rows / 200 ms per transaction) so a backlog dump never holds a write lock long enough to stall reads.

---
```

### User — 2026-09-12T09:22:23.041Z
**result**
```
### Isolates

DuckDB is a single-writer, MVCC embedded engine. One process, so multiple connections against one instance are fine — readers get a snapshot while the writer commits.

- **Writer isolate** — owns ingest, derivation, backfill. Everything that mutates runs here, serialised, in transactions.
- **UI isolate** — read-only connection, queries on demand and on a *debounced* change ping (250 ms). A 3-hour backlog dump must not repaint the fleet list 500 times.

`dart_duckdb` exposes a transferable database handle for exactly this. Ingest batches are bounded (≤ 5k rows / 200 ms per transaction) so a backlog dump never holds a write lock long enough to stall reads.

---
```

### Claude — 2026-09-12T09:24:33.242Z
Ingest pipeline is built and running end to end.

```
lib/core/                     Failure · exceptions · UseCase/StreamUseCase · Result<T> · Clock
lib/features/telemetry_ingest/
  domain/        TelemetryPacket · IngestReceipt · IngestSnapshot · FleetVehicle
                 TelemetryRepository (interface)
                 SeedFleet · ObserveTelemetry · IngestPacketBatch · GetIngestSnapshot
  data/          TelemetryPacketModel · TelemetryPacketSource (interface)
                 SimulatedPacketSource + SimulatorConfig
                 TelemetryWriter (long-lived writer isolate)
                 TelemetryLocalDataSource + DuckDb impl · TelemetryRepositoryImpl
  presentation/  IngestController (GetxController) · IngestBinding · IngestPage
```

`domain` imports nothing outward. Concrete classes are named in exactly one place — `IngestBinding`. Every function has a doc comment explaining why, not what.

**Simulator** — seeded, so a run replays exactly. Injects all four failure modes the brief names: retransmits (identical event time, delivered a tick or two later, so dedupe is tested *across* batches not just within one), out-of-order delivery (held back, original event time kept), drops, and basement backlogs (buffered while dark, dumped in one burst). Signals report on different cadences, so packets carry a subset — which is why freshness is per-signal.

**Writer** — spawned once and kept; `Isolate.run` per batch costs more than the write. Batches bulk-append into two TEMP staging tables via DuckDB's row-wise appender (no statement per row, no escaping vehicle ids into SQL), then everything is set-based: `DISTINCT ON` for intra-batch duplicates, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` for duplicates already on disk, the conditional upsert for late packets. Two transactions, watermark last, exactly as §3.4 specifies.

**Verified:** 33 tests pass, `flutter analyze` clean, macOS and Android both build. The real macOS app ran for 18s and left 40 vehicles / 4269 signal rows / 627 fixes / 240 latest-value rows / 40 watermarks on disk — read back by querying the app's actual database file, not from the UI.

The test that carries the design: `ingest_pipeline_test.dart` replays the same feed reversed, in ragged batches, with every third packet redelivered — log, latest, fixes and watermarks come back byte-identical to the in-order run.

Two things to flag. `conn.append(table, 'temp.main')` segfaults dart_duckdb (`unique_ptr is NULL`); the schema argument must be `null` for TEMP tables — found by probe, not by guessing. And the ingest monitor auto-starts the feed, since a screen whose job is showing the pipeline work shouldn't need a click.

**AI logs** exported to `ai-logs/` — 2 sessions, 419 turns, tool calls and results included. The exporter needed `--src`/`--match` flags: Claude Code groups transcripts by launch directory, and these sessions were started from a parent folder, so they sat beside unrelated work. `--match byte_beam_project` keeps only this project's conversations; I checked the output for cross-project content and found none.

ARCHITECTURE.md §1 is rewritten — it previously specified Riverpod and argued *against* repository interfaces. Your instruction overrides that, so the doc now documents what the code does; worth knowing the doc changed position, since you'll defend it live. Everything uncommitted.

Outranks everything: if we\n  /// have not heard from the truck, nothing else we know about it is a claim\n  /// worth making.\n  offline,\n\n  /// Fresh speed above zero.\n  moving,\n\n  /// Fresh speed of zero with fresh ignition on.\n  idle,\n\n  /// Ignition off \u2014 and the documented fallback when the deciding signals are\n  /// too old to judge.\n  stopped;\n\n  /// Parses the string the SQL `CASE` produces.\n  static VehicleStatus fromSql(String value) => switch (value) {\n        'OFFLINE' => VehicleStatus.offline,\n        'MOVING' => VehicleStatus.moving,\n        'IDLE' => VehicleStatus.idle,\n        'STOPPED' => VehicleStatus.stopped,\n        _ => throw ArgumentError('unknown status: $value'),\n      };\n\n  /// The token used in SQL, so the mapping lives in one place.\n  String get sqlName => switch (this) {\n        VehicleStatus.offline => 'OFFLINE',\n        VehicleStatus.moving => 'MOVING',\n        VehicleStatus.idle => 'IDLE',\n        VehicleStatus.stopped => 'STOPPED',\n      };\n\n  /// Label for the status chip.\n  String get label => switch (this) {\n        VehicleStatus.offline => 'Offline',\n        VehicleStatus.moving => 'Moving',\n        VehicleStatus.idle => 'Idle',\n        VehicleStatus.stopped => 'Stopped',\n      };\n}\n\n/// Severity of the worst live threshold breach on a vehicle.\n///\n/// Drives the badge on the fleet list. Today it is computed from\n/// `signal_spec` thresholds against fresh readings; once the alerts feature\n/// owns an alert lifecycle (raise, escalate, dismiss, resolve) the badge reads\n/// open alerts from the `alert` table instead. The thresholds themselves stay\n/// in `signal_spec` either way, so there is one definition, not two.\nenum AlertSeverity {\n  warning,\n  critical;\n\n  static AlertSeverity? fromSql(String? value) => switch (value) {\n        'warning' => AlertSeverity.warning,\n        'critical' => AlertSeverity.critical,\n        _ => null,\n      };\n}\nDART\n\ncat > lib/features/fleet/domain/entities/fleet_filter.dart <<'DART'\nimport 'vehicle_status.dart';\n\n/// The chip row above the fleet list.\n///\n/// [all] is deliberately part of the same enum rather than a nullable status:\n/// the UI needs a fifth chip with its own count, and modelling it as \"no\n/// filter\" would mean a special case at every call site.\nenum FleetFilter {\n  all,\n  moving,\n  idle,\n  stopped,\n  offline;\n\n  /// The status this filter selects, or null for [all].\n  VehicleStatus? get status => switch (this) {\n        FleetFilter.all => null,\n        FleetFilter.moving => VehicleStatus.moving,\n        FleetFilter.idle => VehicleStatus.idle,\n        FleetFilter.stopped => VehicleStatus.stopped,\n        FleetFilter.offline => VehicleStatus.offline,\n      };\n\n  /// Chip label.\n  String get label => switch (this) {\n        FleetFilter.all => 'All',\n        FleetFilter.moving => 'Moving',\n        FleetFilter.idle => 'Idle',\n        FleetFilter.stopped => 'Stopped',\n        FleetFilter.offline => 'Offline',\n      };\n}\nDART\n\ncat > lib/features/fleet/domain/entities/fleet_vehicle_summary.dart <<'DART'\nimport 'vehicle_status.dart';\n\n/// One row of the fleet list.\n///\n/// Every value is nullable except identity and status, because a vehicle that\n/// has never reported a signal is a real case: it shows \"\u2014\" rather than a\n/// fabricated zero.\nclass FleetVehicleSummary {\n  const FleetVehicleSummary({\n    required this.vehicleId,\n    required this.regNo,\n    required this.model,\n    required this.status,\n    this.soc,\n    this.rangeKm,\n    this.speed,\n    this.lastPing,\n    this.alertSeverity,\n  });\n\n  final String vehicleId;\n  final String regNo;\n  final String model;\n\n  /// First-match-wins status, decided in SQL.\n  final VehicleStatus status;\n\n  /// Battery state of charge, %.\n  final double? soc;\n\n  /// Estimated range remaining, km.\n  final double? rangeKm;\n\n  /// Latest speed, km/h. Carried for the list subtitle, not for the status \u2014\n  /// the status was already decided against freshness rules the UI does not\n  /// re-implement.\n  final double? speed;\n\n  /// Newest event time from any signal or position report.\n  final DateTime? lastPing;\n\n  /// Worst live threshold breach, or null when nothing is wrong.\n  final AlertSeverity? alertSeverity;\n\n  /// True when the vehicle has never reported anything.\n  bool get hasNeverReported => lastPing == null;\n}\nDART\n\ncat > lib/features/fleet/domain/entities/fleet_overview.dart <<'DART'\nimport 'fleet_filter.dart';\nimport 'fleet_vehicle_summary.dart';\n\n/// Everything the fleet screen renders in one value.\n///\n/// Rows and counts travel together because they are read in the same refresh\n/// and must agree: a chip that says \"12 moving\" beside a list of 9 is worse\n/// than either number being slightly stale.\nclass FleetOverview {\n  const FleetOverview({\n    required this.filter,\n    required this.vehicles,\n    required this.counts,\n  });\n\n  const FleetOverview.empty()\n      : filter = FleetFilter.all,\n        vehicles = const [],\n        counts = const {};\n\n  /// Which chip produced [vehicles].\n  final FleetFilter filter;\n\n  /// The rows matching [filter], already ordered.\n  final List<FleetVehicleSummary> vehicles;\n\n  /// Live count per chip, computed in SQL over the whole fleet \u2014 not by\n  /// counting [vehicles], which only holds the current filter.\n  final Map<FleetFilter, int> counts;\n\n  /// Count for one chip, zero when no vehicle is in that state.\n  int countFor(FleetFilter filter) => counts[filter] ?? 0;\n\n  /// True when the fleet has vehicles but this filter matched none \u2014 the case\n  /// that gets an empty state rather than a blank screen.\n  bool get isFilteredEmpty =>\n      vehicles.isEmpty && countFor(FleetFilter.all) > 0;\n\n  /// True when there is no fleet at all yet.\n  bool get isFleetEmpty => countFor(FleetFilter.all) == 0;\n}\nDART\ndart format lib/features/fleet >/dev/null && dart analyze lib/features/fleet 2>&1 | tail -3",

No scan of the\n/// multi-million-row log happens here, which is the entire reason\n/// `vehicle_signal_latest` is maintained on ingest.\nabstract class FleetLocalDataSource {\n  /// Rows for [filter] plus the count for every chip, read at instant [now].\n  Future<FleetOverview> overview(FleetFilter filter, DateTime now);\n}\n\n/// DuckDB implementation.\nclass DuckDbFleetLocalDataSource implements FleetLocalDataSource {\n  const DuckDbFleetLocalDataSource(this._read);\n\n  /// The UI isolate's read connection. Sees an MVCC snapshot, so a commit in\n  /// the writer never blocks the list.\n  final Connection _read;\n\n  @override\n  Future<FleetOverview> overview(FleetFilter filter, DateTime now) async {\n    try {\n      // Two queries, both over the same CTE: the counts must span the whole\n      // fleet while the rows are narrowed to one chip. Counting the returned\n      // rows instead would make every chip read \"the number you can see\".\n      final counts = await _counts(now);\n      final vehicles = await _vehicles(filter, now);\n      return FleetOverview(filter: filter, vehicles: vehicles, counts: counts);\n    } catch (error) {\n      throw LocalDatabaseException('fleet overview query failed', error);\n    }\n  }\n\n  /// Count per status, plus the total for the \"All\" chip.\n  Future<Map<FleetFilter, int>> _counts(DateTime now) async {\n    final result = await _query(\n      '$_scoredCte SELECT status, count(*) FROM scored GROUP BY status',\n      now,\n    );\n\n    final counts = {for (final filter in FleetFilter.values) filter: 0};\n    var total = 0;\n    for (final row in result.fetchAll()) {\n      final status = VehicleStatus.fromSql(row[0]! as String);\n      final count = (row[1]! as num).toInt();\n      total += count;\n      counts[_filterFor(status)] = count;\n    }\n    counts[FleetFilter.all] = total;\n    return counts;\n  }\n\n  /// The rows for one chip, ordered by registration so the list is stable\n  /// between refreshes \u2014 an order that jumps as speeds change is unreadable.\n  Future<List<FleetVehicleSummaryModel>> _vehicles(\n    FleetFilter filter,\n    DateTime now,\n  ) async {\n    final status = filter.status;\n    final where = status == null ? '' : \"WHERE status = '${status.sqlName}'\";\n    final result = await _query(\n      '$_scoredCte SELECT vehicle_id, reg_no, model, status, soc, range_km, '\n      'speed, last_ping, alert_severity FROM scored $where ORDER BY reg_no',\n      now,\n    );\n    return [\n      for (final row in result.fetchAll()) FleetVehicleSummaryModel.fromRow(row),\n    ];\n  }\n\n  /// Runs a statement built on [_scoredCte], binding the instant to compare\n  /// freshness against.\n  ///\n  /// `now` is bound rather than taken from SQL's own `now()` so tests can pin\n  /// it \u2014 a fleet list whose rules depend on the wall clock is untestable.\n  Future<ResultSet> _query(String sql, DateTime now) async {\n    final statement = await _read.prepare(sql);\n    try {\n      statement.bindNamedParams({'now': now});\n      return await statement.execute();\n    } finally {\n      await statement.dispose();\n    }\n  }\n\n  /// Maps a status back to the chip that selects it.\n  FleetFilter _filterFor(VehicleStatus status) => switch (status) {\n        VehicleStatus.moving => FleetFilter.moving,\n        VehicleStatus.idle => FleetFilter.idle,\n        VehicleStatus.stopped => FleetFilter.stopped,\n        VehicleStatus.offline => FleetFilter.offline,\n      };\n}\n\n/// How long without any report before a vehicle counts as offline.\nconst _offlineAfter = 'INTERVAL 10 MINUTE';\n\n/// The shared prefix: every fleet query selects from `scored`.\n///\n/// Four stages, each earning its place:\n///\n/// * `latest`  \u2014 pivots the long-format latest table into one row per vehicle.\n///   `FILTER` keeps it to a single pass, and the per-signal timestamps come\n///   along because freshness is judged per signal, not per vehicle.\n/// * `ping`    \u2014 vehicle-level last ping is the newest event time from *any*\n///   signal or position report (\u00a710, ambiguity 2).\n/// * `spec`    \u2014 thresholds and staleness windows read from `signal_spec`, so\n///   they are configuration rather than constants buried in this string.\n/// * `scored`  \u2014 the first-match-wins status and the alert badge.\n///\n/// Two things in the status ladder are deliberate. Every branch below OFFLINE\n/// tests its own signal's freshness first, so a vehicle that is online but\n/// whose speed is 40 minutes old cannot claim MOVING. And STOPPED is the\n/// `ELSE`: it is both \"ignition off\" and the documented fallback when the\n/// deciding signals are too stale to judge (\u00a710, ambiguity 1).\nconst _scoredCte = '''\nWITH latest AS (\n  SELECT vehicle_id,\n         max(value)    FILTER (WHERE signal = 'soc')          AS soc,\n         max(event_ts) FILTER (WHERE signal = 'soc')          AS soc_ts,\n         max(value)    FILTER (WHERE signal = 'range_km')     AS range_km,\n         max(value)    FILTER (WHERE signal = 'speed')        AS speed,\n         max(event_ts) FILTER (WHERE signal = 'speed')        AS speed_ts,\n         max(value)    FILTER (WHERE signal = 'ignition')     AS ignition,\n         max(event_ts) FILTER (WHERE signal = 'ignition')     AS ignition_ts,\n         max(value)    FILTER (WHERE signal = 'battery_temp') AS battery_temp,\n         max(event_ts) FILTER (WHERE signal = 'battery_temp') AS battery_temp_ts,\n         max(event_ts)                                        AS last_signal_ts\n  FROM vehicle_signal_latest\n  GROUP BY vehicle_id\n),\nping AS (\n  SELECT vehicle_id, max(event_ts) AS last_ping\n  FROM (\n    SELECT vehicle_id, last_signal_ts AS event_ts FROM latest\n    UNION ALL\n    SELECT vehicle_id, max(event_ts) AS event_ts FROM location_fix GROUP BY vehicle_id\n  )\n  GROUP BY vehicle_id\n),\nspec AS (\n  SELECT max(max_age_sec) FILTER (WHERE signal = 'speed')        AS speed_age,\n         max(max_age_sec) FILTER (WHERE signal = 'ignition')     AS ignition_age,\n         max(max_age_sec) FILTER (WHERE signal = 'soc')          AS soc_age,\n         max(max_age_sec) FILTER (WHERE signal = 'battery_temp') AS battery_temp_age,\n         max(warn_lo)     FILTER (WHERE signal = 'soc')          AS soc_warn_lo,\n         max(crit_lo)     FILTER (WHERE signal = 'soc')          AS soc_crit_lo,\n         max(crit_hi)     FILTER (WHERE signal = 'battery_temp') AS temp_crit_hi\n  FROM signal_spec\n),\nscored AS (\n  SELECT v.vehicle_id,\n         v.reg_no,\n         v.model,\n         l.soc,\n         l.range_km,\n         l.speed,\n         p.last_ping,\n         CASE\n           WHEN p.last_ping IS NULL OR p.last_ping < \\$now - $_offlineAfter\n             THEN 'OFFLINE'\n           WHEN l.speed_ts >= \\$now - to_seconds(s.speed_age) AND l.speed > 0\n             THEN 'MOVING'\n           WHEN l.speed_ts >= \\$now - to_seconds(s.speed_age) AND l.speed = 0\n            AND l.ignition_ts >= \\$now - to_seconds(s.ignition_age)\n            AND l.ignition = 1\n             THEN 'IDLE'\n           ELSE 'STOPPED'\n         END AS status,\n         CASE\n           WHEN l.battery_temp_ts >= \\$now - to_seconds(s.battery_temp_age)\n            AND l.battery_temp > s.temp_crit_hi THEN 'critical'\n           WHEN l.soc_ts >= \\$now - to_seconds(s.soc_age)\n            AND l.soc < s.soc_crit_lo THEN 'critical'\n           WHEN l.soc_ts >= \\$now - to_seconds(s.soc_age)\n            AND l.soc < s.soc_warn_lo THEN 'warning'\n           ELSE NULL\n         END AS alert_severity\n  FROM vehicle v\n  LEFT JOIN latest l USING (vehicle_id)\n  LEFT JOIN ping p USING (vehicle_id)\n  CROSS JOIN spec s\n)\n''';\nDART\n\ncat > lib/features/fleet/data/models/fleet_vehicle_summary_model.dart <<'DART'\nimport '../../domain/entities/fleet_vehicle_summary.dart';\nimport never learns about column order.\nclass FleetVehicleSummaryModel extends FleetVehicleSummary {\n  const FleetVehicleSummaryModel({\n    required super.vehicleId,\n    required super.regNo,\n the fleet query selects.\n  factory FleetVehicleSummaryModel.fromRow(List<Object?> row) {\n    return FleetVehicleSummaryModel(\n      vehicleId: row[0]! as String,\n      regNo: row[1]! as String,\n      model: row[2]! as String,\n      status: VehicleStatus.fromSql(row[3]! as String),\n      soc: (row[4] as num?)?.toDouble(),\n      rangeKm: (row[5] as num?)?.toDouble(),\n      speed: (row[6] as num?)?.toDouble(),\n      lastPing: row[7] as DateTime?,\n      alertSeverity: AlertSeverity.fromSql(row[8] as String?),\n    );\n  }\n}\nDART\ndart format lib/features/fleet >/dev/null && dart ### Claude — 2026-09-12T20:04:22.413Z
Fleet home is built. Feature A is done.

```
lib/features/fleet/
  domain/        VehicleStatus · AlertSeverity · FleetFilter
                 FleetVehicleSummary · FleetOverview
                 FleetRepository · GetFleetOverview
  data/          FleetLocalDataSource (the SQL) + DuckDb impl
                 FleetVehicleSummaryModel · FleetRepositoryImpl
  presentation/  FleetController · FleetBinding · FleetPage
                 StatusChip/AlertBadge · FleetFilterBar · VehicleTile · FleetEmptyState
lib/core/db/database_pulse.dart
```

Fleet is now home; the ingest monitor moved behind an app-bar action.

**Status and counts are both SQL.** One `scored` CTE — pivot `vehicle_signal_latest` with `FILTER`, union in `location_fix` for the vehicle-level last ping, cross-join thresholds from `signal_spec` — then two queries over it: `GROUP BY status` for the chips, `WHERE status = …` for the rows. Counts span the whole fleet, never just what's on screen; there's a test for that, because counting the returned rows is the easy bug.

**A test forced a design correction.** I had the status ladder checking each signal against its own `signal_spec.max_age_sec` (5 min). But OFFLINE is a 10-minute window, so that leaves a dead band: a truck reporting every six minutes is online yet every signal is stale, and it reads STOPPED while visibly driving. The ladder now scores freshness against the same 10-minute window — status is a vehicle-level claim, so it gets the vehicle's liveness window. The alert badge keeps the per-signal window, since the brief scopes thresholds to fresh readings and a threshold is a claim about one signal. That's logged as ambiguity 14 in ARCHITECTURE.md, and ambiguity 1 now names which window it meant.

**Refresh** goes through `DatabasePulse` — the writer bumps a counter after commit, the fleet controller debounces it at 250 ms. A backlog dump commits several batches back to back and repaints once; there's a test that eight pings produce one reload.

**Verified:** 73 tests pass (20 SQL status/badge/chip cases, 5 controller, 7 widget, 8 end-to-end), `flutter analyze` clean, macOS and Android both build. The end-to-end test runs simulator → writer isolate → DuckDB → fleet query with nothing stubbed; I also ran the real macOS app and pointed the fleet query at the database it left behind: 40 vehicles, 14 moving / 13 idle / 13 stopped / 0 offline summing to 40, 4 badges on the low-battery trucks the simulator plants.

I could not take a screenshot — `screencapture` is blocked by screen-recording permission for this terminal, so the visual check is the widget tests plus that real-data query, not my eyes on the window. Grant permission if you want an actual capture.

skipped: tapping a row does nothing yet — vehicle detail is the next feature and `VehicleTile` already takes an `onTap`. AI logs refreshed; everything uncommitted.

Null means the signal has never\n  /// reported, which gets no pill at all rather than a fourth verdict.\n  static ReadingVerdict? fromSql(String? value) => switch (value) {\n        'NORMAL' => ReadingVerdict.normal,\n        'ALERT' => ReadingVerdict.alert,\n        'STALE' => ReadingVerdict.stale,\n        _ => null,\n      };\n\n  /// Pill label.\n  String get label => switch (this) {\n        ReadingVerdict.normal => 'NORMAL',\n        ReadingVerdict.alert => 'ALERT',\n        ReadingVerdict.stale => 'STALE',\n      };\n}\nDART\n\ncat > lib/features/vehicle_detail/domain/entities/signal_reading_row.dart <<'DART'\nimport 'reading_verdict.dart';\n\n/// One row of the readings register.\nclass SignalReadingRow {\n  const SignalReadingRow({\n    required this.signal,\n    required this.label,\n    required this.unit,\n    required this.maxAge,\n    this.value,\n    this.eventTs,\n    this.verdict,\n  });\n\n  /// Signal key, e.g. `soc`.\n  final String signal;\n\n  /// Human label, read from `signal_spec` rather than hard-coded here.\n  final String label;\n\n  /// Unit suffix. value;\n\n  /// When that value was measured.\n  final DateTime? eventTs;\n\n  /// Null when the signal has never reported \u2014 that row shows \"\u2014\" and no pill.\n  final ReadingVerdict? verdict;\n\n  /// True when nothing has ever arrived for this signal.\n  bool get hasNeverReported => eventTs == null;\n\n  /// Age of the reading at [now].\n  Duration? ageAt(DateTime now) =>\n      eventTs == null ? null : now.difference(eventTs!);\n}\nDART\n\ncat > lib/features/vehicle_detail/domain/entities/soc_history.dart <<'DART'\n/// One plotted point: a time bucket and the mean SOC inside it.\nclass SocPoint {\n  const SocPoint({required this.at, required this.value});\n\n  /// Earliest event time in the bucket.\n  final DateTime at;\n\n  /// Mean state of charge across the bucket, %.\n  final double value;\n}\n\n/// Battery history over the queried window.\n///\n/// Carries [readingCount] alongside [points] deliberately: the chart is a\n/// bucketed summary, and showing how many raw log rows it came from is the\n/// honest way to present it \u2014 and the visible proof that this screen queries\n/// the event log rather than the latest-value table.\nclass SocHistory {\n  const SocHistory({\n    required this.points,\n    required this.readingCount,\n    required this.from,\n    required this.to,\n  });\n\n  const SocHistory.empty()\n      : points = const [],\n        readingCount = 0,\n        from = null,\n        to = null;\n\n  /// Bucketed series, oldest first.\n  final List<SocPoint> points;\n\n  /// Raw `signal_reading` rows the buckets were built from.\n  final int readingCount;\n\n  /// Oldest and newest event time in the window, or null when empty.\n  final DateTime? from;\n  final DateTime? to;\n\n  bool get isEmpty => points.isEmpty;\n\n  /// Lowest plotted value, for the chart's axis and the summary line.\n  double get min => points.map((p) => p.value).reduce((a, b) => a < b ? Future<VehicleDetail?> detail(\n    String vehicleId,\n    DateTime now, {\n    Duration window,\n    int maxPoints,\n  });\n}\n\n/// DuckDB implementation.\nclass DuckDbVehicleDetailLocalDataSource implements VehicleDetailLocalDataSource {\n  const DuckDbVehicleDetailLocalDataSource(this._read);\n\n  final Connection _read;\n\n  @override\n  Future<VehicleDetail?> detail(\n    String vehicleId,\n    DateTime now, {\n    Duration window = const Duration(hours: 24),\n    int maxPoints = 180,\n  }) async {\n    try {\n      final header = await _header(vehicleId, now);\n      if (header == null) return null;\n\n      return VehicleDetail(\n        vehicleId: vehicleId,\n        regNo: header.$1,\n        model: header.$2,\n        status: header.$3,\n        lastPing: header.$4,\n        readings: await _register(vehicleId, now),\n        history: await _socHistory(vehicleId, now, window, maxPoints),\n      );\n    } catch (error) {\n      throw LocalDatabaseException('vehicle detail query failed', error);\n    }\n  }\n\n  /// Registration, model, status and last ping \u2014 the same `scored` CTE the\n  /// fleet list uses, narrowed to one row.\n  Future<(String, String, VehicleStatus, DateTime?)?> _header(\n    String vehicleId,\n    DateTime now,\n  ) async {\n    final result = await _query(\n      '$scoredCte SELECT reg_no, model, status, last_ping FROM scored '\n      'WHERE vehicle_id = \\$vehicle',\n      now,\n      vehicleId: vehicleId,\n    );\n    final row = result.fetchOne();\n    if (row == null) return null;\n    return (\n      row[0]! as String,\n      row[1]! as String,\n      VehicleStatus.fromSql(row[2]! as String),\n      row[3] as DateTime?,\n    );\n  }\n\n  /// One row per configured signal, whether or not it has ever reported.\n  ///\n  /// Driven from `signal_spec` with a LEFT JOIN, so adding a signal to the\n  /// config table adds a register row with no code change \u2014 and a signal that\n  /// has never arrived still gets a row, which is the \"\u2014, no pill\" case the\n  /// brief calls for.\n  Future<List<SignalReadingRowModel>> _register(\n    String vehicleId,\n    DateTime now,\n  ) async {\n    final result = await _query(\n      '''\n      SELECT s.signal, s.label, s.unit, s.max_age_sec, l.value, l.event_ts,\n             CASE\n               WHEN l.event_ts IS NULL THEN NULL\n               WHEN l.event_ts < \\$now - to_seconds(s.max_age_sec) THEN 'STALE'\n               WHEN (s.crit_lo IS NOT NULL AND l.value < s.crit_lo)\n                 OR (s.crit_hi IS NOT NULL AND l.value > s.crit_hi)\n                 OR (s.warn_lo IS NOT NULL AND l.value < s.warn_lo)\n                 OR (s.warn_hi IS NOT NULL AND l.value > s.warn_hi) THEN 'ALERT'\n               ELSE 'NORMAL'\n             END AS verdict\n      FROM signal_spec s\n      LEFT JOIN vehicle_signal_latest l\n        ON l.signal = s.signal AND l.vehicle_id = \\$vehicle\n      ORDER BY list_position($_registerOrder, s.signal)\n      ''',\n      now,\n      vehicleId: vehicleId,\n    );\n    return [\n      for (final row in result.fetchAll()) SignalReadingRowModel.fromRow(row),\n    ];\n  }\n\n  /// Battery history, bucketed down to at most [maxPoints] points.\n  ///\n  /// This is the one query that touches the multi-million-row event log, and\n  /// it is the point of the section: the latest-value table could not answer\n  /// it. Bucketing happens in SQL \u2014 pulling a day of raw readings into Dart to\n  /// thin them there would defeat the exercise.\n  Future<SocHistory> _socHistory(\n    String vehicleId,\n    DateTime now,\n    Duration window,\n    int maxPoints,\n  ) async {\n    // Bucket width is derived from the window so the series is bounded no\n    // matter how chatty the vehicle is. `epoch()` division needs no extension\n    // and no date-truncation function.\n    final bucketSeconds = (window.inSeconds / maxPoints).ceil().clamp(1, 86400);\n    final from = now.subtract(window);\n\n    final result = await _query(\n      '''\n      SELECT min(event_ts) AS at, avg(value) AS value, count(*) AS n\n      FROM signal_reading\n      WHERE vehicle_id = \\$vehicle AND signal = 'soc' AND event_ts >= \\$from\n      GROUP BY floor(epoch(event_ts) / $bucketSeconds)\n      ORDER BY at\n      ''',\n      now,\n      vehicleId: vehicleId,\n      from: from,\n    );\n\n    final points = <SocPoint>[];\n    var readings = 0;\n    for (final row in result.fetchAll()) {\n      points.add(SocPoint(\n        at: row[0]! as DateTime,\n        value: (row[1]! as num).toDouble(),\n      ));\n      readings += (row[2]! as num).toInt();\n    }\n\n    return SocHistory(\n      points: points,\n      readingCount: readings,\n      from: points.isEmpty ? null : points.first.at,\n      to: points.isEmpty ? null : points.last.at,\n    );\n  }\n\n  /// Binds the parameters every query here shares.\n  Future<ResultSet> _query(\n    String sql,\n    DateTime now, {\n    required String vehicleId,\n    DateTime? from,\n  }) async {\n    final statement = await _read.prepare(sql);\n    try {\n      statement.bindNamedParams({\n        'now': now,\n        'vehicle': vehicleId,\n        if (from != null) 'from': from,\n      });\n      return await statement.execute();\n    } finally {\n      await statement.dispose();\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n/// Register display order.\n///\n/// A list in SQL rather than a `sort_order` column: ordering is a presentation\n/// concern that does not justify a migration, and `list_position` keeps it to\n/// one expression. It is not a milder alert \u2014 it is a refusal to\n/// judge, and giving it a warning colour would make \"we do not know\" look like\n/// \"something is slightly wrong\".\nclass VerdictPill extends StatelessWidget {\n  const VerdictPill({required this.verdict, super.key});\n\n  final ReadingVerdict? verdict;\n\n  @override\n  Widget build(BuildContext context) {\n    final value = verdict;\n    if (value == null) return const SizedBox.shrink();\n\n    final scheme = Theme.of(context).colorScheme;\n    final (background, foreground) = switch (value) {\n      ReadingVerdict.normal => (scheme.primaryContainer, scheme.onPrimaryContainer),\n      ReadingVerdict.alert => (scheme.errorContainer, scheme.onErrorContainer),\n      ReadingVerdict.stale => (\n          scheme.surfaceContainerHighest,\n          scheme.outline,\n        ),\n    };\n\n    return Container(\n      padding: const EdgeInsets.symmetric(horizontal: 8, vertical: 3),\n      decoration: BoxDecoration(\n        color: background,\n        borderRadius: BorderRadius.circular(6),\n      ),\n      child: Text(\n        value.label,\n        style: Theme.of(context).textTheme.labelSmall?.copyWith(\n              color: foreground,\n              fontWeight: FontWeight.w700,\n              letterSpacing: 0.4,\n            ),\n      ),\n    );\n  }\n}\nDART\n\ncat > lib/features/vehicle_detail/presentation/widgets/reading_row_tile.dart <<'DART'\nimport 'package:flutter/material.dart';\n\nimport '../../domain/entities/signal_reading_row.dart';\nimport 'verdict_pill.dart';\n\n/// One row of the readings register: label, value, its own age, and a verdict.\nclass ReadingRowTile extends StatelessWidget {\n  const ReadingRowTile({\n    required this.row,\n    required this.now,\n    super.key,\n  });\n\n  final SignalReadingRow row;\n\n  /// The instant the verdict was computed against. Ages are rendered from the\n  /// same reading so a pill and its age can never contradict each other.\n  final DateTime now;\n\n  @override\n  Widget build(BuildContext context) {\n    final theme = Theme.of(context);\n    return Padding(\n      padding: const EdgeInsets.symmetric(horizontal: 16, vertical: 12),\n      child: Row(\n        children: [\n          Expanded(\n            flex: 4,\n            child: Column(\n              crossAxisAlignment: CrossAxisAlignment.start,\n              children: [\n                Text(row.label, style: theme.textTheme.bodyMedium),\n                Text(\n                  _age(),\n                  style: theme.textTheme.bodySmall?.copyWith(\n                    color: theme.colorScheme.outline,\n                  ),\n                ),\n              ],\n            ),\n          ),\n          Expanded(\n            flex: 3,\n            child: Text(\n              _value(),\n              textAlign: TextAlign.right,\n              style: theme.textTheme.titleMedium?.copyWith(\n                fontFeatures: const [FontFeature.tabularFigures()],\n              ),\n            ),\n          ),\n          const SizedBox(width: 12),\n          SizedBox(width: 72, child: Align(\n            alignment: Alignment.centerRight,\n            child: VerdictPill(verdict: row.verdict),\n          )),\n        ],\n      ),\n    );\n  }\n\n  /// An em dash, never a zero: a signal that has never reported has no value,\n  /// and \"0%\" would read as an empty battery.\n  String _value() {\n    final value = row.value;\n    if (value == null) return '\u2014';\n    // Ignition is a boolean riding in a DOUBLE column; rendering it as \"1.0\"\n    // would be technically true and useless.\n    if (row.signal == 'ignition') return value >= 0.5 ? 'On' : 'Off';\n    final decimals = row.signal == 'odometer' ? 0 : 1;\n    return '${value.toStringAsFixed(decimals)}${row.unit.isEmpty ? '' : ' ${row.unit}'}';\n  }\n\n  /// Age of this reading, phrased at the coarsest useful precision.\n  String _age() {\n    final age = row.ageAt(now);\n    if (age == null) return 'never reported';\n    return '${formatAge(age)} ago \u00b7 stale after ${formatAge(row.maxAge)}';\n  }\n}\n\n/// Compact duration formatting shared by the register and the header.\nString formatAge(Duration age) {\n  if (age.inSeconds < 60) return '${age.inSeconds}s';\n  if (age.inMinutes < 60) return '${age.inMinutes}m';\n  if (age.inHours < 48) return '${age.inHours}h';\n  return '${age.inDays}d';\n}\nDART\ndart format lib >/dev/null && dart analyze lib 2>&1 | tail -3",

   child: Padding(\n            padding: const EdgeInsets.symmetric(horizontal: 16),\n            child: CustomPaint(\n              size: Size.infinite,\n              painter: _SparklinePainter(\n                values: [for (final point in history.points) point.value],\n                line: theme.colorScheme.primary,\n                fill: theme.colorScheme.primary.withValues(alpha: 0.12),\n                grid: theme.colorScheme.outlineVariant,\n              ),\n            ),\n          ),\n        ),\n      ],\n    );\n  }\n}\n\n/// Draws the series against a fixed 0\u2013100% final List<double> values;\n  final Color line;\n  final Color fill;\n  final Color grid;\n\n  @override\n  void paint(Canvas canvas, Size size) {\n    // A fixed axis, not one scaled to the data. Auto-scaling would make a\n    // battery drifting between 61% and 63% look like a cliff.\n    const minValue = 0.0;\n    const maxValue = 100.0;\n\n    final gridPaint = Paint()\n      ..color = grid\n      ..strokeWidth = 1;\n    for (final fraction in [0.0, 0.5, 1.0]) {\n      final y = size.height * fraction;\n      canvas.drawLine(Offset(0, y), Offset(size.width, y), gridPaint);\n    }\n\n    if (values.length < 2) return;\n\n    final dx = size.width / (values.length - 1);\n    double yFor(double value) =>\n        size.height * (1 - (value - minValue) / (maxValue - minValue));\n\n    final path = Path()..moveTo(0, yFor(values.first));\n    for (var i = 1; i < values.length; i++) {\n      path.lineTo(dx * i, yFor(values[i]));\n    }\n\n    final area = Path.from(path)\n      ..lineTo(size.width, size.height)\n      ..lineTo(0, size.height)\n      ..close();\n    canvas.drawPath(area, Paint()..color = fill);\n\n    canvas.drawPath(\n      path,\n      Paint()\n        ..color = line\n        ..style = PaintingStyle.stroke\n        ..strokeWidth = 2\n        ..strokeJoin = StrokeJoin.round,\n    );\n  }\n\n  @override\n  bool shouldRepaint(_SparklinePainter old) =>\n      old.values != values || old.line != line;\n}\nDART\n\ncat > lib/features/vehicle_detail/presentation/pages/vehicle_detail_page.dart <<'DART'\nimport    body: Obx(() {\n        if (_controller.error.isNotEmpty) {\n          return Center(child: Text(_controller.error.value));\n        }\n        if (_controller.isLoading.value) {\n          return This asserts\n/// the thing those tests could not \u2014 that building the binding actually\n/// starts the pipeline and rows reach the disk.\nvoid main() {\n  setUpAll(useHostDuckDb);\n\n  late FleetDb db;\n\n  setUp(() async {\n    db = await FleetDb.open(':memory:');\n    Get.put(DatabasePulse(), permanent: true);\n  });\n\n  tearDown(() async {\n    await Get.find<TelemetryLocalDataSource>().dispose();\n    await Get.reset();\n    await db.close();\n  });\n\n  testWidgets('building the binding seeds the roster and starts the feed',\n      (tester) async {\n    await IngestBinding(\n      db: db,\n      clock: const SystemClock(),\n      config: const SimulatorConfig(\n        vehicleCount: 5,\n        tick: Duration(milliseconds: 20),\n      ),\n    ).dependenciesAsync();\n\n    final controller = Get.find<IngestController>();\n    expect(controller.isRunning.value, isTrue, reason: 'nobody opened a screen');\n    expect(controller.snapshot.value.vehicles, 5);\n\n    // Let a few ticks land.\n    await tester.pump(const Duration(milliseconds: 200));\n    await tester.runAsync(\n      () => Future<void>.delayed(const Duration(milliseconds: 400)),\n    );\n\n    expect(controller.batchesWritten.value, greaterThan(0));\n    final onDisk = await db.read.query('SELECT count(*) FROM signal_reading');\n    expect((onDisk.fetchOne()!.first as num).toInt(), greaterThan(0),\n        reason: 'the feed ran but nothing reached the database');\n  });\n}\nDART\ndart format test >/dev/null && flutter test test/features/telemetry_ingest/ingest_binding_test.dart 2>&1 | tr '\\r' '\\n' | grep -E 'All tests|Some tests|Expected:|Actual:|\\[E\\]|reason|Which' | head -12",

  "timeout": 300000
}

  setUpAll(useHostDuckDb);

  late FleetDb db;

  setUp(() async {
    db = await FleetDb.open(':memory:');
    Get.put(DatabasePulse(), permanent: true);
  });

  tearDown(() async {
    await Get.find<TelemetryLocalDataSource>().dispose();
    Get.reset();
    await db.close();
  });

  testWidgets('building the binding seeds the roster and starts the feed', (
    tester,
  ) async {
    await IngestBinding(
      db: db,
      clock: const SystemClock(),
      config: const SimulatorConfig(
        vehicleCount: 5,
        tick: Duration(milliseconds: 20),
      ),
    ).dependenciesAsync();

      controller.isRunning.value,

    );

    // Let a few ticks land.
    await Future<void>.delayed(const Duration(milliseconds: 400));
    await controller.stop();

import 'package:flutter/material.dart';
import 'package:get/get.dart';
import 'package:path/path.dart' as p;
import 'package:path_provider/path_provider.dart';

import 'core/db/database_pulse.dart';
import 'db/fleet_db.dart';
import 'features/fleet/presentation/bindings/fleet_binding.dart';
import 'features/fleet/presentation/pages/fleet_page.dart';
import 'features/telemetry_ingest/presentation/bindings/ingest_binding.dart';
import 'features/vehicle_detail/presentation/bindings/vehicle_detail_binding.dart';

/// Opens the database and builds the object graph before the first frame.
///
/// Deliberately not lazy. Opening DuckDB and spawning the writer isolate are
/// both async, and a screen that builds before its writer exists would have to
/// carry a "not ready yet" state that never means anything useful.
Future<void> main() async {
  WidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized();

  final db = await FleetDb.open(await databasePath());
  Get.put<FleetDb>(db, permanent: true);
  Get.put(DatabasePulse(), permanent: true);

  await IngestBinding(db: db).dependenciesAsync();
  FleetBinding(db: db).dependencies();
  VehicleDetailBinding(db: db).dependencies();

  runApp(const FleetConsoleApp());
}

/// Where the database lives. Application support, not documents or cache:
/// it is app-private state the OS must not evict.
Future<String> databasePath() async {
  final dir = await getApplicationSupportDirectory();
  await dir.create(recursive: true);
  return p.join(dir.path, 'fleet.duckdb');
}

/// Root widget. `GetMaterialApp` rather than `MaterialApp` so routes and
```

{
  "command": "cd /Users/durgeshparekh/Documents/GitHub/byte_beam_project\npython3 - <<'PY'\nimport pathlib\np = pathlib.Path(\"lib/features/vehicle_detail/data/datasources/vehicle_detail_local_data_source.dart\")\ns = p.read_text()\nold_start = s.index(\"  /// Battery history, bucketed down to at most [maxPoints] points.\")\nold_end = s.index(\"  /// Prepares [sql] and binds [params] positionally.\")\nnew = '''  /// Battery history, bucketed down to at most [maxPoints] points.\n  ///\n  /// This is the one query that touches the multi-million-row event log, and\n  /// it is the point of the section: the latest-value table could not answer\n  /// it. Bucketing happens in SQL \u2014 pulling a day of raw readings into Dart to\n  /// thin them there would defeat the exercise.\n  Future<SocHistory> _socHistory(\n    String vehicleId,\n    DateTime now,\n    Duration window,\n    int maxPoints,\n  ) async {\n    final from = now.subtract(window);\n\n    // Bucket width comes from the span the data actually covers, not from the\n    // requested window. A vehicle with twenty seconds of history inside a\n    // 24-hour window would otherwise collapse into a single point.\n    final span = await _query(\n      '''\n      SELECT min(event_ts), max(event_ts)\n      FROM signal_reading\n      WHERE vehicle_id = \\\\$1 AND signal = 'soc' AND event_ts >= \\\\$2\n      ''',\n      [vehicleId, from],\n    );\n    final spanRow = span.fetchOne()!;\n    if (spanRow[0] == null) return const SocHistory.empty();\n\n    final covered = (spanRow[1]! as DateTime).difference(spanRow[0]! as DateTime);\n    final bucketSeconds = (covered.inSeconds / maxPoints).ceil().clamp(1, 86400);\n\n    final result = await _query(\n      '''\n      SELECT min(event_ts) AS at, avg(value) AS value, count(*) AS n\n      FROM signal_reading\n      WHERE vehicle_id = \\\\$1 AND signal = 'soc' AND event_ts >= \\\\$2\n      GROUP BY floor(epoch(event_ts) / $bucketSeconds)\n      ORDER BY at\n      ''',\n      [vehicleId, from],\n    );\n\n    final points = <SocPoint>[];\n    var readings = 0;\n    for (final row in result.fetchAll()) {\n      points.add(SocPoint(\n        at: row[0]! as DateTime,\n        value: (row[1]! as num).toDouble(),\n      ));\n      readings += (row[2]! as num).toInt();\n    }\n\n    return SocHistory(\n      points: points,\n      readingCount: readings,\n      from: points.isEmpty ? null : points.first.at,\n      to: points.isEmpty ? null : points.last.at,\n    );\n  }\n\n'''\np.write_text(s[:old_start] + new + s[old_end:])\nPY\ndart format lib >/dev/null && flutter analyze 2>&1 | tail -2 && flutter test test/features/vehicle_detail/ 2>&1 | tr '\\r' '\\n' | grep -E 'All tests|Some tests|Expected|Actual|\\[E\\]' | head -8",
  "description": "Derive bucket width from the actual data span",

}

[ARCHITECTURE.md](../ARCHITECTURE.md)\ncovers the system: the event-log model, the offline-first ownership split, the\ningest contract, the scale plan, and the ambiguity table. These documents cover\nwhat was actually built and why it looks the way it does.\n\n| # | Feature | Brief | Status |\n|---|---------|-------|--------|\n| 01 | [Telemetry ingest](01-telemetry-ingest.md) | \u00a72 local-first over DuckDB | Built |\n| 02 | [Fleet home](02-fleet-home.md) | \u00a73 A | Built |\n| 03 | [Vehicle detail](03-vehicle-detail.md) | \u00a73 B | Built |\n| \u2014 | Alerts, dismissal, undo | \u00a73 C | Not built |\n| \u2014 | Geofences | \u00a73 D | Not built |\n| \u2014 | Automatic trips | \u00a73 E | Not built |\n| \u2014 | Scale exercise | \u00a74 | Not built |\n\nEvery document follows the same shape: what it does, the files, the data flow,\nthe mechanics, the decisions with their rejected alternatives, the tests that\npin each claim, and what is knowingly left undone.\n\n## Conventions used throughout\n\n**Clean architecture per feature.** `domain` holds entities, repository\ninterfaces and use cases and imports nothing outward. `data` holds models, data\nsources and repository implementations. `presentation` holds a GetX controller,\na binding and widgets. The concrete classes are named in exactly one place per\nfeature \u2014 its `Bindings`.\n\n**GetX for state and injection.** Controllers receive use cases through their\nconstructors rather than calling `Get.find` internally, so every controller is\ndirectly constructible in a unit test with fakes.\n\n**Event time, never arrival time.** Every rule in the app compares `event_ts`\n\u2014 when the vehicle measured something \u2014 against an injected clock. `ingest_ts`\nexists for audit and drives nothing.\n\n**Failures, not exceptions, above the data layer.** Data sources throw\n`LocalDatabaseException`; repositories catch it and return `Err(Failure)`.\nNothing in `domain` or `presentation` catches anything.\nMD\necho written",

{
  "command": "cd /Users/durgeshparekh/Documents/GitHub/byte_beam_project\ncat > docs/01-telemetry-ingest.md <<'MD'\n# 01 \u2014 Telemetry ingest\n\nTakes packets from a flaky link and gets them onto disk exactly once, in a way\nthat survives duplicates, reordering, dropped packets and hours-long backlogs.\n\nCovers the brief's \u00a72 (local-first over DuckDB) and supplies the data every\nother feature reads.\n\n## Files\n\n```\nlib/features/telemetry_ingest/\n  domain/entities/         telemetry_packet \u00b7 ingest_receipt \u00b7 ingest_snapshot \u00b7 fleet_vehicle\n  domain/repositories/     telemetry_repository\n  domain/usecases/         seed_fleet \u00b7 observe_telemetry \u00b7 ingest_packet_batch \u00b7 get_ingest_snapshot\n  data/models/             telemetry_packet_model (+ ingest_receipt_model)\n  data/datasources/        telemetry_packet_source (interface)\n                           simulated_packet_source + simulator_config\n                           telemetry_writer_isolate\n                           telemetry_local_data_source (interface + DuckDB impl)\n  data/repositories/       telemetry_repository_impl\n  presentation/            ingest_controller \u00b7 ingest_binding \u00b7 ingest_page\nlib/core/db/database_pulse.dart\n```\n\n## Data flow\n\n```\nSimulatedPacketSource \u2500\u2500batch\u2500\u2500\u25b6 IngestController \u2500\u2500\u25b6 IngestPacketBatch\n  (Timer, UI isolate)                                      \u2502\n                                                           \u25bc\n                                            TelemetryRepositoryImpl\n                                                           \u2502\n                                            DuckDbTelemetryLocalDataSource\n                                                           \u2502\n                                          \u250c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2534\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2510\n                                          \u25bc                               \u25bc\n                                  TelemetryWriter                   db.read\n                                (writer isolate)                (UI isolate, MVCC)\n                                          \u2502                               \u25b2\n                                          \u25bc                               \u2502\n                                       DuckDB \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500 DatabasePulse.ping \u2518\n```\n\n## The writer\n\nDuckDB permits one writer, so every mutation in the app funnels through one\nlong-lived isolate. It is spawned once at startup and kept \u2014 `Isolate.run` per\nbatch would cost more than the write it performs. Each request carries its own\nreply port, which removes request-id bookkeeping entirely.\n\nReads use the UI isolate's own connection. DuckDB is MVCC, so a read sees a\nconsistent snapshot while the writer commits; the fleet list never blocks on a\nbacklog dump.\n\n## One batch, step by step\n\nThe batch lands in two TEMP staging tables through DuckDB's row-wise appender \u2014\nno statement per row, no parameter binding, no escaping vehicle ids into SQL\ntext. Everything after that is set-based.\n\n**Transaction 1 \u2014 the log and the latest values**\n\n```sql\nINSERT INTO signal_reading\nSELECT vehicle_id, signal, event_ts, value, CAST(now() AS TIMESTAMP)\nFROM (SELECT DISTINCT ON (vehicle_id, signal, event_ts) * FROM staging_signal\n      ORDER BY vehicle_id, signal, event_ts)\nON CONFLICT DO NOTHING;\n```\n\n`DISTINCT ON` collapses duplicates *within* the batch. `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`\nagainst the natural primary key `(vehicle_id, signal, event_ts)` collapses them\nagainst everything already on disk. Re-delivering a packet is a no-op enforced\nby the database, not by application code \u2014 which is why at-least-once delivery\nis all this pipeline ever needs.\n\n```sql\nINSERT INTO vehicle_signal_latest AS l\nSELECT vehicle_id, signal, event_ts, value\nFROM (SELECT DISTINCT ON (vehicle_id, signal) * FROM staging_signal\n      ORDER BY vehicle_id, signal, event_ts DESC)\nON CONFLICT (vehicle_id, signal) DO UPDATE\n  SET value = excluded.value, event_ts = excluded.event_ts\n  WHERE excluded.event_ts > l.event_ts;\n```\n\nThat `WHERE` is the entire late-packet story for the read path. A packet that\narrives late but measures an older moment is appended to the log and leaves the\ncurrent value alone.\n\n**Transaction 2 \u2014 the derivation position**\n\nCounts the vehicles whose batch reaches back behind the watermark (the replays\ngeofences and trips will need), then advances the watermark, guarded the same\nway so a late batch cannot drag it backwards.\n\nSplitting the two keeps a long recompute from holding a write lock across the\nwhole batch, and enforces the ordering that matters: **the watermark advances\nlast, so derived state is always behind the log and never ahead of it.** A\ncrash between the two leaves log rows with no derivation \u2014 which is identical\nto the state during normal operation and is repaired by the same \"derive from\nwatermark\" call every batch makes. There is no recovery routine.\n\n## The simulator\n\nA fake fleet on a deliberately bad link. Every random decision comes from one\nseeded generator, so a run replays exactly and a failing test can be repeated.\n\n| Fault | How it is produced | What it tests |\n|---|---|---|\n| Retransmit | The same packet queued for release a tick or two later | Cross-batch dedupe, not just the easy within-batch case |\n| Out of order | Packet held back, original `event_ts` kept | The conditional upsert and the late-vehicle counter |\n| Loss | Packet never emitted | That gaps are tolerated rather than interpolated |\n| Backlog | Vehicle goes dark for N ticks, buffers, then dumps in one burst | Bounded batches, and the UI surviving a burst |\n\nVehicles report signals on different cadences \u2014 speed and ignition every tick,\nSOC every second, battery temperature every third, odometer every fourth \u2014 so a\npacket carries a *subset* of signals. That is not decoration: it is the reason\nfreshness has to be tracked per signal rather than per vehicle. Every seventh\ntruck starts near the low-battery threshold and every eleventh runs hot, so the\nthreshold paths have material without waiting for a lucky random walk.\n\n## Decisions\n\n| Decision | Why | Rejected |\n|---|---|---|\n| Natural-key PK on the log | Makes idempotency a database constraint instead of a code path, so at-least-once delivery suffices | Dedupe in Dart against a recent-packet set \u2014 correct until the set is evicted |\n| Staging tables + appender | One `INSERT \u2026 SELECT` per target instead of a statement per row; no SQL string building around vehicle ids | Multi-row `VALUES` built by string concatenation |\n| Two transactions per batch | Short write locks, and derived state can only lag the log | One transaction \u2014 a long recompute would block readers for its whole duration |\n| Long-lived writer isolate | Spawn cost paid once | `Isolate.run` per batch |\n| Location in its own table | A fix is a triple, not a scalar; pairing lat/lon rows by timestamp is pointless work | `lat` and `lon` as two signals in the log |\n| Start the pipeline from the binding | It must run whether or not its monitor screen is mounted, and `main` can `await` it before `runApp` | `onInit` \u2014 a hook that cannot be awaited, so the first frame could race the roster seed |\n\n## Tests\n\n| Claim | Test |\n|---|---|\n| A redelivered packet applies nothing | `ingest_pipeline_test.dart` |\n| Duplicates inside one batch collapse before the log | same |\n| A late packet lands in the log but never clobbers the latest value | same |\n| A batch behind the watermark is reported late; the watermark never reverses | same |\n| **Any shuffling or repetition of a feed produces identical final state** | same \u2014 the invariant the whole design rests on |\n| The simulator actually injects all four faults, and replays under one seed | `simulated_packet_source_test.dart` |\n| Counters fold receipts correctly; failures surface without killing the feed | `ingest_controller_test.dart` |\n| Building the binding really starts the pipeline and rows reach disk | `ingest_binding_test.dart` |\n\nThat last one exists because every other test passed while a running app\ningested nothing. Unit tests cannot see wiring.\n\n## Known limits\n\n* No backend, so no outbox \u2014 see ARCHITECTURE.md \u00a73.7. The cursor mechanism is\n  real because the simulator is a positioned, replayable source.\n* The watermark advances with no derivation behind it yet. Derived tables are\n  class D \u2014 dropped and rebuilt \u2014 so the first derivation pass rebuilds from\n  the log regardless.\n* `DatabasePulse` is a counter, not a change feed. It says \"something was\n  written\", not what; every reader re-queries. Fine at this scale, and the\n  place to look first if refresh cost ever matters.\nMD\nwc -l docs/01-telemetry-ingest.md",
  "description": "Write the ingest feature documentation"
}

A list of every vehicle with registration, model, SOC,\nrange, an alert badge and a status chip, above filter chips carrying live\ncounts.\n\nCovers the brief's \u00a73 A.\n\n## Files\n\n```\nlib/db/vehicle_status_sql.dart          the status ladder, shared\nlib/features/fleet/\n  domain/entities/         vehicle_status (+ alert_severity) \u00b7 fleet_filter\n                           fleet_vehicle_summary \u00b7 fleet_overview\n  domain/repositories/     fleet_repository\n  domain/usecases/         get_fleet_overview\n  data/models/             fleet_vehicle_summary_model\n  data/datasources/        fleet_local_data_source (interface + DuckDB impl)\n  data/repositories/       fleet_repository_impl\n  presentation/            fleet_controller \u00b7 fleet_binding \u00b7 fleet_page\n  presentation/widgets/    status_chip (+ alert_badge) \u00b7 fleet_filter_bar\n                           vehicle_tile \u00b7 fleet_empty_state\n```\n\n## What the query touches\n\n`vehicle` (500 rows) joined to `vehicle_signal_latest` (500 \u00d7 6) and\n`signal_spec` (6). **The multi-million-row event log is never scanned here.**\nThat is the whole reason `vehicle_signal_latest` is maintained on ingest, and\nwhy the fleet list stays fast as the log grows.\n\n## The status ladder\n\nLives in `lib/db/vehicle_status_sql.dart`, beside the schema rather than inside\nthis feature, because it is a property of the data and not of a screen \u2014 the\nvehicle detail header makes the same claim about the same vehicle, and two\ncopies of the `CASE` would eventually disagree.\n\nFour CTEs:\n\n* `latest` \u2014 pivots the long-format latest table into one row per vehicle with\n  `FILTER`, carrying each signal's own timestamp.\n* `ping` \u2014 vehicle-level last ping is the newest event time from *any* signal\n  **or position report**.\n* `spec` \u2014 thresholds and staleness windows read from `signal_spec`, so they\n  are configuration rather than constants buried in a SQL string.\n* `scored` \u2014 the first-match-wins status and the alert badge.\n\n```sql\nCASE\n  WHEN p.last_ping IS NULL OR p.last_ping < $1 - INTERVAL 10 MINUTE THEN 'OFFLINE'\n  WHEN l.speed_ts    >= $1 - INTERVAL 10 MINUTE AND l.speed > 0     THEN 'MOVING'\n  WHEN l.speed_ts    >= $1 - INTERVAL 10 MINUTE AND l.speed = 0\n   AND l.ignition_ts >= $1 - INTERVAL 10 MINUTE AND l.ignition = 1  THEN 'IDLE'\n  ELSE 'STOPPED'\nEND\n```\n\nEach branch below OFFLINE checks its own signal's freshness first, so a vehicle\nthat is online but whose speed is 40 minutes old cannot claim MOVING. STOPPED\nis the `ELSE`: both \"ignition off\" and the documented fallback when the\ndeciding signals are too stale to judge.\n\n### Two freshness windows, on purpose\n\nThe ladder scores freshness against the **10-minute OFFLINE window**. The alert\nbadge scores it against the **per-signal `signal_spec.max_age_sec`** (5 minutes\nfor SOC and battery temperature).\n\nThey differ because the claims differ in scope. Status is a vehicle-level\nclaim, so its inputs get the vehicle's own liveness window; a threshold is a\nclaim about one signal, and the brief scopes thresholds to fresh readings.\n\nThis was a correction, not a plan. Scoring the ladder against the 5-minute\nwindow leaves a dead band between 5 and 10 minutes in which a truck that is\nonline and visibly moving reads STOPPED, because every signal is stale while\nthe vehicle is not yet offline. A test caught it. It is written up as ambiguity\n14 in ARCHITECTURE.md.\n\n## Counts\n\n```sql\n<scored CTE> SELECT status, count(*) FROM scored GROUP BY status\n```\n\nCounts span the whole fleet; rows are a second query over the same CTE with\n`WHERE status = \u2026`. Counting the returned rows instead would make every chip\nread \"the number you can already see\", which is the easy bug here and has its\nown test.\n\n## The alert badge\n\nComputed from `signal_spec` thresholds against fresh readings: SOC below 10 %\nor battery over 45 \u00b0C is critical, SOC below 20 % is a warning.\n\nThis is a placeholder with a defined end. Once the alerts feature owns an alert\nlifecycle \u2014 raise, escalate, dismiss, resolve \u2014 the badge reads open alerts\nfrom the `alert` table instead. The thresholds stay in `signal_spec` either\nway, so there is one definition throughout, and only the \"which vehicles have\nsomething wrong\" query moves.\n\n## Refresh\n\nThe writer bumps `DatabasePulse` after each commit; the controller debounces it\nat 250 ms. A vehicle leaving a basement commits several batches back to back,\nand the list only needs to land once.\n\nSwitching a chip re-queries rather than filtering in memory \u2014 the counts are\nSQL-side and a chip's row set is a different query, not a subset of one already\nheld.\n\n## Decisions\n\n| Decision | Why | Rejected |\n|---|---|---|\n| Status ladder in shared SQL | Two screens make the same claim | A copy per feature, or a Dart helper (moves the fold into memory) |\n| Counts and rows as two queries | Chips must count the fleet, not the page | One query plus in-memory grouping |\n| Positional `$1` binding | DuckDB 1.2.1 cannot resolve a *second* named parameter in a statement that opens with `WITH` | Named parameters \u2014 they fail at run time, not compile time |\n| `all` inside `FleetFilter` | The UI needs a fifth chip with its own count | A nullable status, which special-cases every call site |\n| Order by registration | A list that reorders as speeds change is unreadable | Order by status or SOC |\n| Two distinct empty states | \"No vehicle is moving\" and \"no telemetry at all\" need different words | One generic empty widget |\n| Em dash, never zero | A fabricated `0%` reads as an empty battery | Coalescing nulls to zero in SQL |\n\n## Tests\n\n| Claim | Test |\n|---|---|\n| Each rung of the ladder, including both stale-signal fallthroughs and the 9- and 6-minute boundaries | `fleet_query_test.dart` |\n| Badge thresholds, including that a stale low battery raises nothing | same |\n| Counts span the fleet while rows narrow to the chip | same |\n| Filtered-empty is distinguishable from fleet-empty | same |\n| One reload per burst of writes, not one per write | `fleet_controller_test.dart` |\n| Chips render their counts; rows render values, dashes and pills; tapping switches filter | `fleet_page_test.dart` |\n| Simulator \u2192 writer isolate \u2192 DuckDB \u2192 fleet query, nothing stubbed; every vehicle lands in exactly one chip; the whole fleet reads offline 11 minutes later | `fleet_end_to_end_test.dart` |\n\n## Known limits\n\n* The badge is threshold-derived, not alert-derived (see above).\n* Counts and rows are two round trips. Both are small; if it ever matters they\n  merge into one query returning rows plus a window-function count.\n* No search, no sort control, no pagination. 500 rows in a `ListView.separated`\n  is fine; a larger fleet wants `LIMIT`/`OFFSET`, which the query already\n  supports shape-wise.\nMD\nwc -l docs/02-fleet-home.md",
  "description": "Write the fleet home documentation"
}

{
